# RELAY: Lablo IPTV v1 to store submission

**Objective:** build Lablo IPTV v1 for Samsung Tizen and LG webOS as approved in [docs/PLAN.md](docs/PLAN.md), up to the first store submissions (LG in the US and GCC, Samsung in the US).
**Source:** the planning conversation of 2026-11-01 (leg 0). Plan: [docs/PLAN.md](docs/PLAN.md) (it wins on conflicts). Detailed design: [docs/architecture.md](docs/architecture.md). Research: [docs/research/](docs/research/). Session breakdown follows architecture.md section 6, plus the v1 additions in PLAN.md section 4.

This file replaces the `ROADMAP.md` that PLAN.md mentions: these steps are the per-session checklist.

## Definition of done (global DoD applied to Lablo)
1. **Tests and build:** `pnpm verify` is green (lint incl. boundaries, typecheck, tests, both TV builds, ES2016 syntax gate, bundle budgets; each part counts once it exists).
2. **PR and CI:** a PR to `main` on private repo `Omarmarei/Lablo` with green CI, merged with a merge commit (not squash, so the long-lived `relay/<run>` branch stays mergeable).
   - **Standing approval from Omar (2026-11-01):** the relay merges its own PRs (about one per phase) once CI is green, and lists the 3 riskiest changes in that leg's handover. Production deploys and migrations still need his word. Step 1 copies this rule into CLAUDE.md.
3. **Migrations:** N/A until step 29. After that, Neon production migrations are applied and the schema is checked with a query. Each one is `[needs Omar]`.
4. **Deploy:**
   - **Backend (Vercel):** each production deploy is `[needs Omar]`; verify the live endpoint afterwards.
   - **TV app:** the "deploy" is a store submission (`[needs Omar]`). Before that, check the change installed on a real TV once the test TVs exist.
5. **Handover** last.

UI steps also end with a short "press these keys" checklist that Omar can run in the desktop browser (`pnpm dev`) or on a TV.

### Phase 0: Foundations
- [x] 0. Research, plan, architecture docs (leg 0). Commit `4581e54`.

  - ESLint flat config with `eslint-plugin-boundaries` (import rules from architecture.md 1.1; `player` must NOT import `platform-api`, while `platform-api` may import `player` types). `no-restricted-globals` for `tizen`/`webapis`/`webOS`/`PalmSystem` outside `platform-*`.
  - Vitest, plus a test proving the boundary lint rejects a forbidden import.
  - `CLAUDE.md`: conventions, Chromium 53 cheat sheet, boundaries, DoD, merge policy, and "never fetch provider URLs server-side / never relay streams".
  - `docs/adr/ADR-001..007`:
    1. packaged apps
    2. Preact + signals + custom focus engine
    3. chrome53 IIFE + syntax gate
    4. native players first
    5. servers never touch provider URLs or streams
    6. E2E pairing and backup crypto
    7. Stalker dropped
  - GitHub Actions CI running `pnpm verify`; README.
  - The private repo `Omarmarei/Lablo` was created in leg 0 and `main` pushed. Push the relay branch, open the first PR, and merge it on green CI.
  - **Done when:** `pnpm verify` is green locally and on CI, and the lint test fails on a seeded forbidden import.

  - `tools/build/build-tv.ts`: Rollup IIFE, Babel `preset-env {chrome:53}`, core-js, workers inlined as Blob URLs.

- [x] 2b. **Old-engine test harness (the main substitute for missing TVs).** Done in leg 3, commit `e632376`. `pnpm verify:engines [--self-test]` (`tools/engines`, runbook docs/runbooks/old-engines.md).
  - Pinned snapshots 403380 (53.0.2785), 433042 (56.0.2924) and 561732 (68.0.3440; the suggested 550428 is M67).
  - The engines run headed under Xvfb in a **Debian 9** container (on Debian 12, Chromium 53/56 segfault on a HarfBuzz ABI clash) and are driven over raw CDP.
  - Self-test: the ES2018 seed fails on 53/56 and passes on 68; the ES2020 seed and the no-polyfills build fail on all three; the real app passes on all. CI job `engines`, separate from `verify`.
  - **Finding for S1:** IndexedDB in a Blob worker on `file://` is denied on desktop 53–68 without file access. The harness runs with `--allow-file-access-from-files` (as the TV runtimes do); S1 must confirm on the TVs.
  - Script that fetches Chromium snapshot builds matching the TV engines (Chromium 53 = webOS 4, 56 = Tizen 4, 68/69 = 2020 sets), e.g. from `chromium-browser-snapshots`.
  - Run the built TV bundle headless in each, through an old-protocol driver (a matching old Puppeteer or chromedriver). Assert boot with no syntax or runtime errors, a remote-key navigation smoke test, and IDB plus Web Worker sanity.
  - Wire it into CI (Linux snapshots) as part of `pnpm verify:engines`.
  - **Done when:** CI fails on a seeded ES2018 feature and on a seeded missing polyfill, and passes on the hello app in all engines.

- [x] 3. **`apps/mock-provider` (2.1).** Done in leg 4, commit `8977442`. `pnpm mock` (runbook docs/runbooks/mock-provider.md). 149 tests; the endpoint test fails if a registered route has no probe, and the fault table is a `Record<FaultType, …>`.
  - Beyond the list: M3U catch-up in every mode (`xc`/`default`/`append`/`shift`/`flussonic`) served with `X-Mock-Archive-*` oracle headers, `json-quirks`/`timezone`/`truncate`/`reset`/`no-cors` faults, `max_connections` counted from open streams, an account with special characters for percent-encoding, runtime scenario switch (`PUT /__mock/scenario`).
  - Media is placeholder bytes behind a `MediaSource` interface (`src/media.ts`); step 4 plugs in the ffmpeg channels and open movies.
  - Fastify.

  - `user_info` states (Active/Expired/Banned/max_connections), base64 short EPG.
  - YAML fault scenarios: 302 chains, stalls with the socket held open, 401/403/404/509, slow headers, malformed M3U, BOM/CRLF, gzip.
  - LAN log collector endpoint.
  - **Done when:** integration tests cover every endpoint and fault.
- [x] 4. **Legal media set and scale fixtures (2.2).** Done in leg 5 (runbook docs/runbooks/mock-provider.md: Media, Scale fixtures, Benchmarks, Docker).
  - `pnpm mock:media` generates 12 live loops plus a generic one, and 15 VOD/episode files plus 2 generic ones, in about 30 s.
  - Live loops are placed on the epoch timeline by a TS rewriter, so live TS, HLS and catch-up are one seamless stream.
  - `pnpm mock:open-movies` fetches the four films with pinned SHA-256, and `/attribution` credits them.
  - `pnpm fixtures` writes the 200k catalog and 50/150/300 MB XMLTV in about 20 s.
  - `pnpm bench` is the harness for steps 5–9. The mock's Dockerfile is in place.
  - CI job `media` runs `pnpm verify:media`: ffprobe on everything the mock serves, and the 2-minute fixture limit.
  - ffmpeg scripts: endless TS and sliding-window HLS test channels with name and clock burned in; multi-audio (ara/eng/5.1 AC3/E-AC3); HEVC 4K; MKV with ara/eng SRT. Plus a Blender open-movies fetch script with an attribution page.
  - `tools/fixtures`: deterministic 200k catalog (M3U + Xtream JSON, ar/en/fr/es names, malformed lines) and 50/150/300 MB XMLTV (+gz).
  - Node benchmark harness; Dockerfile.
  - Needs ffmpeg on PATH. If it's missing, end as needs-you: "install ffmpeg (`winget install Gyan.FFmpeg`)?".
  - **Done when:** `pnpm mock` serves streams that ffprobe validates in a test, and fixtures generate in under 2 minutes.

  - **Done when:** search over the 200k fixture takes ≤ 200 ms in Node and migration tests pass.
  - Done in leg 9. The search index and ranking are in `packages/core/src/search.ts` (architecture.md puts search in core, which also lets the bench and the Chromium 53 bundle check reach it). The rest is in `packages/data/src`:
    - `schema` and `idb`: database version 1, append-only migrations, and callback-driven transactions;
    - `profile`: a versioned document with 3 rolling snapshots;
    - `chunker` and `catalog-store`: 500-item chunks, plus generations (begin, commit, delete, prune);
    - `rpc`: typed calls, cancel tokens, events and transfer;

  - **200k fixture** (`test/search-200k.media.test.ts`): parse → chunker → index → fake-indexeddb → query service. The slowest of 13 queries takes 10.8 ms, and every result is checked against the parsed names.
  - **Migration tests** pin the shape of every database version and upgrade from each earlier version with seeded data. A failing migration leaves the old version intact.
  - **Query worker:** bundled for Chromium 53, it is 100.5 KB (budget 150 KB). It runs with the ES2017 APIs removed and serves search over IndexedDB (`test/query-worker-bundle.test.ts`).
  - **Mutation check:** 16/16 mutants caught.
- [x] 8. **data, part 2 (3.4).** Ingest worker: streamed fetch, DecompressionStream/fflate gunzip, parse, 500-item chunks, generation swap. Busy-pause and low-tier duty cycle; conditional fetch.
  - **Done when:** the crash-mid-ingest test keeps the old generation, and favourites survive a renumbered refresh fixture.
  - Done in leg 10 (`packages/data/src`: `bytes`, `gunzip`, `throttle`, `favourites`, `ingest`, `ingest-service`, `ingest.worker`; architecture.md §1.4 has a "Built in step 8" note). The app wiring and the real-engine run moved to step 8b.
    - **Crash mid-ingest** (`ingest.test.ts`): the database closes under a refresh, as when the worker dies. The old generation still serves exactly the same results, and the next refresh prunes the leftovers and swaps in.
      - Network failures, cancels, HTTP errors, bad gzip and empty playlists also leave the active generation untouched.
    - **Renumbered refresh** (`test/ingest-mock.test.ts`): every Xtream id in the mock moves by 1000, and the favourites follow to the new keys. That includes channel 101, whose same-name, same-tvg-id twin sits in another group.
      - A dropped favourite is kept for 30 days and then removed.
      - Re-matching never guesses between items it can't tell apart.
    - **Conditional fetch:** ETag/If-Modified-Since with 304 (the mock's get.php now sends ETags), plus an FNV-1a byte hash with `INGEST_VERSION` for servers without validators.
    - **Throttle:** busy-pause (expires after 15 s) and the 50/50 low-tier duty cycle.
    - **Worker bundle:** the ingest worker bundles to 128.8 KB and runs in a Chromium 53-like realm against the mock, with gzip through fflate.
      - To fit the 150 KB budget, the TV build no longer injects polyfills for ES2019+ APIs that `lib: ES2017` code can't call, and fflate gets none.
      - That also cut the query worker from 100.5 to 75.8 KB and the main bundle from about 124 to 113.6 KB.
    - **200k playlist** through the real pipeline in Node: about 2.6 s, plain or gzipped (`test/ingest-200k.media.test.ts`).

    - **core** (`epg-match.ts`, `epg-guide.ts`):
      - `EpgWantedSet` and `EpgMatcher`: override, id (case-insensitive; `xtream-id` for Xtream sources), name, then match name (incl. the XMLTV id's stem), with ties flagged and overrides that pin or block. `combineEpgMatches` merges several guides.
      - `EpgGuideReader`: the tokenizer's channel filter, interleaved guides (orphans, capped), the tier window (`EPG_WINDOWS`), UTC minutes, the DST repair, missing stops, 24 h cap and overlap cuts, and the per-channel-day column buckets (`EpgBucketBuilder`, `mergeEpgBuckets`, `epgNowNext` with tvg-shift applied per playlist channel).

    - **Thresholds** (architecture.md §1.5 step 10, derived from S6), enforced by `pnpm bench`, which now exits 1 over a limit:
      - `epg-ingest-low-10pct` ≤ 8 s median on CI (measured 2.5–3 s locally);
      - `epg-ingest-full` ≤ 20 s (measured about 7.5 s);
      - the worst case runs in a 96 MB heap.
    - **Report:** `pnpm epg:report` (files or URLs, gzip sniffed, several guides). The 300 MB fixture gives 14,013 of 20,000 live channels by id (70.1%) and 4,947 of 5,000 guide channels used, and `test/epg-300mb.media.test.ts` checks every number against `catalog.json` in a 96 MB heap. The mock gives 11 of 12 (`test/epg-mock.test.ts`).
    - **Found on the way:**
      - V8 substrings pinned one decoded chunk per channel: the guide ran out of a 160 MB heap. Each guide channel now gets one owned id string (`ownString`).
      - `parseXmltvTime` gives the later instant of a repeated DST hour east of UTC, not the earlier one as ADR-010 said; the reader settles to the earlier one.
      - A lone boundary in the repeated hour can't be decided from ordering. Documented in ADR-010, and pinned by the New York mock test.
    - **Tests:** 28/28 mutants caught (`artifacts/mutate9.py`).
    - PR #11 merged as `fd777e1` (CI `verify`, `engines` and `media` green on `9adcab5`; on the CI runner `epg-ingest-low-10pct` took 2.3 s and `epg-ingest-full` 5.9 s).
- [x] 9b. **EPG (rest of 3.5): storage, refresh and the app.** Leg 13 split the short-EPG fallback out as step 9c.
  - **Storage:** database v2 as an append-only migration, with `epg_buckets` keyed `[epgSrc, gen, channel, day]` and `epg_channels` (the match table and the used guide channels). Extend `schema.test.ts` (pin v2, upgrade from v1 with a seed).
  - **Ingest:** fetch, gunzip and decode as the catalog ingest does (`runIngest`'s transport, `MaybeGunzip`, throttle) into `EpgGuideReader`. Write the buckets about 10 per transaction, merging `merge: true` fragments with the stored record. Then the generation swap, and prune of past days and old generations.
  - **Sources:** the Xtream `xmltv.php` and the M3U `url-tvg` header, automatically; user XMLTV URLs in priority order. The wanted set comes from the stored catalog's live items, with the panel zone (Xtream) or the source's offset as the fallback zone.
  - **Refresh:** every 12 h, or sooner when less than 24 h of future data remains. Low-tier TVs never run EPG and catalog ingest at once (§1.5 step 8).
  - **App:** `DataEngine` gains an EPG refresh and now/next for the query side. The hello screen's catalog panel shows the match rate and a channel's now/next.
  - **Done when:**
    - an EPG refresh of the mock through the data layer (fake-indexeddb) stores exactly the mock's schedule;
    - a crash mid-EPG-ingest keeps the old generation;

  - Done in leg 13 (architecture.md §1.3 and §1.5 "Built in step 9b"; runbooks old-engines.md and tv-build.md).
    - **Storage:** database v2 (`epg_meta`, `epg_buckets`, `epg_channels`), pinned with a seeded upgrade from v1. Typed-array columns round-trip in fake-indexeddb and in Chromium 53/56/68.
    - **Refresh** (`runEpgRefresh` in a new EPG worker, `@lablo/data/epg.worker`):
      - guides from `epgGuides`: the user's first, then Xtream `xmltv.php` and the M3U header's URLs (now stored as `SourceMeta.epgUrls`);
      - the wanted set from every source's active live items;
      - conditional fetch with a basis, so a new catalog re-reads the same bytes;
      - fragments merged into the stored record, also within one batch;
      - swap, previous generation deleted, past days pruned;
      - each guide fails on its own (`failedAt` waits 2 h);
      - `epgRefreshDue`: 12 h, or under 24 h of programmes left, never within 2 h.
    - **App:** `DataEngine.refreshEpg` (one refresh slot shared with the catalog, busy pause, `due`), `nowNext` and `epgStatus` through the query worker. The hello screen's panel shows `epg-status` (match rate) and `nownext-status`.
    - **Found on the way:** with a tvg-shift, core's `epgNowNext` compared an unshifted start with an already-shifted candidate. Fixed and tested.
    - **Workers:** the ingest worker would have been 159.6 KB with the reader, so EPG has its own worker (116.0 KB). Query 80.7 KB, ingest 125.5 KB, main 449.4 KB of 500 (see "Main bundle budget" below).
    - **Tests:** `epg-store`, `epg-ingest` (incl. the crash), `test/epg-ingest-mock.test.ts` (the mock's schedule exactly: M3U `url-tvg`, a gzipped user guide in the low tier, an Xtream guide without offsets in Kathmandu time; now/next for every live channel), DataEngine, e2e (dev and both bundles: 11 of 12 matched), and the old-engine harness: a generated guide read in Chromium 53/56/68 with exact matches and a now/next. 34/34 mutants caught (`artifacts/mutate9b.py`).

- [x] 9c. **Short-EPG fallback (rest of 3.5).**
  - **Data:** for live channels of an Xtream source that no guide matches, `get_short_epg` (core `XtreamClient.shortEpg`, `decodeShortEpg`) through the data layer, cached 30 min per channel. It needs the account, so it runs where the transport is: a `shortEpg` handler on the query worker (measure its budget first) or a short-lived worker.
  - **App:** `DataEngine` asks after a channel has been focused for 600 ms (a debounce; fake timers in tests) and merges the answer into its now/next. The hello screen's panel shows it for an unmatched channel of an Xtream `?source=`.
  - **Done when:** an unmatched mock channel gets now/next from `get_short_epg` after 600 ms of focus and not before, a second focus within 30 min makes no request, and `pnpm verify` is green.
  - Done in leg 14 (architecture.md §1.5 "Built in step 9c"; runbooks mock-provider.md and tv-build.md).
    - **Data:** `QueryService.shortEpg` in the query worker, which now has `fetchTransport`. A channel a guide matches gets the guide's now/next with no request; an unmatched channel of an Xtream panel (an Xtream source, or a `get.php` M3U) gets its panel's `get_short_epg`, kept 30 min per channel (`ShortEpgCache`: 200 channels, empty answers kept, failures not, one request shared). The account comes from the source, the stream id from the stream URL, and the cache is keyed by ContentKey.
    - **Core:** `xtreamEpgProgrammes` turns the entries into the guide's `EpgProgramme` rows (UTC minutes from the timestamps, guessed stops flagged, 24 h cut), and now/next reads them like a stored guide.
    - **App:** `DataEngine.focusChannel(channel | null)`: 600 ms (`FOCUS_MS`), a new focus cancels the waiting one, and a late answer is dropped. The hello screen's panel has a "Panel EPG" row (`shortepg-status`, `data-from`): it focuses the first live channel no guide matches.
    - **Size:** `XtreamClient` would have made the query worker 110.5 KB, so the short-EPG call and its plumbing moved to core `xtream-epg.ts` and `xtream-base.ts` (re-exported by `xtream.ts`), and two polyfills were avoided (`JSON.stringify` via `parseContentKey`, `padStart` in `Fnv1a`). Query 89.8 KB (+9.1), main 459.1 KB of 500 (tizen; +9.7), EPG 115.1 KB, ingest 124.9 KB.
    - **Mock:** scenario `no-xmltv` (the panel's guide off, `get_short_epg` still on).
    - **Tests:** `short-epg.test.ts`, the conversion in `xtream.test.ts`, `test/short-epg-mock.test.ts` (the "done when", through `DataEngine` with fake `setTimeout` and the mock over a socket), the bundled query worker asking with `fetch`, and e2e (dev and both bundles). 26/26 mutants caught (`artifacts/mutate9c.py`).
    - Local gates: `pnpm verify` green (946 tests in 70 files, both TV builds, e2e 7/7); `pnpm verify:engines --self-test`: all 24 runs as expected on Chromium 53/56/68, 16 seeded failures caught.
    - PR #13 merged as `bbb197e` (CI `verify` 4 m 22 s, `engines` 2 m 49 s and `media` 9 m 32 s green on `6089ab7`).

**Main bundle budget (decide before step 11).** The TV build inlines every worker into `main.js`, and the 500 KB main budget counts them. With the EPG worker and the short EPG, main was 459.1 KB (step 9c); with the focus engine it is 470.3 KB (step 10), so the rest of the UI (design system, i18n, screens) has only about 30 KB. Options for Omar when that runs out:
1. count inlined workers against their own 150 KB budgets only, and keep the 500 KB for the main thread's own code (recommended: a worker string is not parsed as code until the worker starts);
2. ship the workers as separate files in the package (needs spike S1 to show `file://` workers on the TVs);
3. raise the main budget.

**Decided by Omar on 2026-11-03 (in leg 15's tab): option 1.**
- Each inlined worker counts only against its own 150 KB budget.
- The 500 KB main budget covers the main thread's own code: about 103 KB today, that is main.js minus the four worker strings.
- He also authorised the matching edit to the CLAUDE.md `build:tv` row.
- Applied in leg 16 (`tools/build`, the first commit of the step-11 PR): workers go into main.js as placeholders and their code replaces them after terser, so the build knows the inlined bytes exactly.
  - The build prints `main.js (tizen): 102.2 KB own code of 500.0 KB (20%), plus 368.1 KB of inlined workers` (webos 100.8 KB); the files stay 470.3 and 468.9 KB.
  - The workers' own 150 KB checks are unchanged. ADR-003, CLAUDE.md's `build:tv` row and runbook tv-build.md say so.

- [x] 10. **Focus engine (4.1).** Intents, zones (`list`/`grid`/`epg`/`free`), RTL horizontal flip, per-zone memory, router/back stack, `rootBackPolicy`, Magic Remote hover-to-focus.
  - **Done when:** unit tests pass in LTR and RTL.
  - Done in leg 15 (architecture.md §1.2 "Built in step 10"; runbooks tv-build.md "Desktop check" and old-engines.md).
    - **Engine** (`packages/ui/src/focus`): framework-free, with `subscribe` (one notification per change); the app wraps it in a signal. `moveDirection` is the one RTL swap (LEFT is `end` in RTL, UP/DOWN never swap), and zones, neighbours and free-zone rects are logical, so nothing swaps twice.
    - **Zones:** `list` (vertical/horizontal, `wrap`, a `visible` window with a minimal-move `top` for virtual lists), `grid` (short last row), `epg` (row plus a time cursor in UTC minutes: UP/DOWN keep it, LEFT/RIGHT go cell to cell, slots where a row has no data, view by slots), `free` (logical rects, gap plus twice the sideways offset). Neighbours skip empty zones; per-zone memory; CH_UP/CH_DOWN page; `Screen.update` keeps positions that still fit.
    - **Router:** root given at creation, `push`/`replace`/`reset`/`pop`; covered screens keep their positions; Back pops, and on the root follows `rootBackPolicy` (`exit-dialog` pushes the app's exit screen, `platform-back` calls `exitApp`). Screens see every Intent first (`onIntent`) and get OK (`onSelect`).
    - **Magic Remote:** `Screen.hover` focuses only on the top screen and while the pointer is in use; a key hides it and the focus stays; `watchPointerMoves` counts only moves to new coordinates (Chromium's still-pointer mousemoves after a scroll are ignored); new `Platform.onPointer` (webOS `cursorStateChange`, none elsewhere); `wheel` scrolls the focused zone and keeps the focus in view (non-passive, the page never scrolls).
    - **App:** the hello screen's Focus block (rail, a 4-row list window, a 3-column grid; OK opens details, More goes deeper; the exit dialog on Tizen and in the browser), `dir` from the device locale.
    - **Found on the way:** with a cold Vite cache (as in CI), Vite found `fflate` late (only the ingest worker imports it) and reloaded every open page in the middle of e2e tests; `vite.config.ts` now pre-bundles it.
    - **Size:** main 459.1 → 470.3 KB of 500 (tizen; about 8 KB engine, 3 KB demo, no new polyfills). Workers unchanged.

Done and ↓ move the focus down, ↑ up; the text is always kept. ### Hardware lab
Omar has **one LG TV and no Samsung TV** (2026-11-01). Testing relies on several layers:
1. The old-engine harness (step 2b).
2. The Tizen TV Emulator/Simulator and the webOS Emulator/Simulator, for APIs, lifecycle and keys only (no real media).
3. Omar's LG TV, for real webOS media.
4. A Samsung real-device option that Omar chooses at step 16.

  - **The build:** `pnpm build:tv --lab` stages `dist/tv-lab/{tizen,webos}`: its own entry, app id and title (installs next to the app), the lab server's address fixed at build time (default: this PC's LAN address, port 8080). `apps/tv/lab.html` runs it under `pnpm dev`.
  - **The runner:** checks grouped by spike; one session per run; each record (`spike`, `check`, `status`, `value`, `ms`) posted as NDJSON to the collector (`POST /__lab/logs/<session>`, retried) and kept on `window.__LABLO_LAB__`; a screen driven by keys (run all, each spike's results).
  - **The spikes:**
    - S0: the device facts;
    - S1: the runtime (polyfills, Preact, Blob worker, `response.body` streaming, TextDecoder, an IndexedDB bulk write, IndexedDB in the Blob worker);
    - S2: storage (markers in localStorage and IndexedDB read back on the next run, with the version that wrote them; the quota; a write test up to 200 MB);
    - S3: device IDs (Tizen DUID, webOS LGUDID through the adapters, hashed, compared across runs);
    - S7: cross-origin `http://` calls (no CORS headers, a redirect, `responseURL`).
  - **Done when:** e2e runs the lab in the desktop browser against the in-process mock and finds its records in the collector; the lab bundle runs in Chromium 53/56/68 (`verify:engines`) with S1 passing; PR merged.
  - Done in leg 27 (runbook docs/runbooks/lab.md; architecture.md §5 "Built in step 15a"):
    - **Build:** `pnpm build:tv` builds the app and the lab (`--target app|lab`); the lab goes to `dist/tv-lab/{tizen,webos}` with ids `com.lablo.iptv.lab` / `LabloLab01.LabloLab`, a native-feature probe before the bundle, and `lab-config.js` (`--lab-server`, default this PC's first private IPv4 on port 8080; `--lab-version`, `--lab-write-mb`, `--lab-no-auto`). It runs every spike at launch. `pnpm device install|launch webos tv --lab`. Lab main 131.9 KB own code plus a 33 KB worker. The app's main grew 0.3 KB (tizen) and 1.5 KB (webos).
    - **Runner:** checks with timeouts (a throw is a fail, never a stop); records `start`/`check`/`end` posted as NDJSON (≤ 50 per request, in order, retried after 1, 2, 4… s, max 30 s, never dropped) and kept on `window.__LABLO_LAB__`; a screen by keys (Run all, OK on a spike runs it, the focused spike's checks).
    - **Spikes:** S0 device; S1 (10 checks: polyfills against the native probe, APIs and CSS, Preact, Blob worker, TextDecoder over every split, `response.body` on the page and in the worker, IDB, a 10,000-item bulk write in the worker); S2 (markers read back by the next run with their version, quota, a 200 MB write test); S3 (`Platform.deviceSecret()`, new: Tizen `getDuid()`, webOS luna `deviceid/getIDs` over `PalmServiceBridge` (lint-restricted to the adapter), hash and format only; `stable`); S7 (XHR/fetch with and without CORS, a 302 with `responseURL`/`response.url`; `Response.redirected` is Chromium 57+, so only reported).
    - **Mock:** credential-free probes `/__lab/bytes`, `/__lab/echo?cors=0`, `/__lab/redirect` (only `/__lab/` targets).

- [x] 22a. **The refresh schedule in the running app.** A scheduler in the app (`apps/tv/src/data/refresh-scheduler.ts`):
  - **When:** a first check 15 s after the live screen opens (the start on the last channel and the first list stay fast), then every 15 min, and 15 s after the app is visible again.
  - **Catalog** (architecture.md §1.4 item 1; no step owned it): each stored source whose last check is 12 h old is refreshed, one at a time. A failure waits 2 h (kept in memory).
  - **Guides:** `refreshEpg(..., { due: true })` for every source's guides and the user's. After a catalog refresh that committed, every guide is read (new channels may match).
  - **One at a time:** the data engine's refresh slot (a low-tier TV never runs both); a slot held by another refresh (onboarding, the hello screen) moves the work to the next check.
  - **The screens pick it up:** the live screen re-asks now/next after a guide swaps in (rows keep their old answer until the new one comes); after a catalog swap it reads its categories again quietly, on the same category and the same focused channel. The player keeps playing, and finds its channel's row (and the previous channel's) in the new list.

    - unit tests: the scheduler (the delays, the 12 h rule, failures, the slot, the guides after a commit, hidden and visible, dispose), the live screen (now/next re-asked after a guide swap, stale answers dropped; the category and focus kept after a catalog swap), the player (its row found again, no new play);

- [x] 24b. **The time offset per guide.**
  - **Decided (not a gate):** one setting per guide, an offset of −12 h to +12 h in 30-minute steps added to every time it gives, for the user's guides and the sources' own (`xmltv.php`, `url-tvg`). It is applied when the query worker reads (as tvg-shift is), not at ingest: a change shows at once with no download, which matters on a TV where reading a big guide again takes minutes and a user tries +1 h, then +2 h. The ingest-time `shiftMinutes` goes, so no guide is shifted twice.
  - **Data:** the offsets in the profile (optional, no migration), by guide id; the query worker reads them at each reload, and an edit reloads it. Now/next, the grid's rows and the details all use them.

    - **The guides' channels:** each generation stores every XMLTV channel its guide declares (`[epgSrc, gen, 'declared']` beside the match table: ids, the shown names and a normalized search text built in the EPG worker; no migration; pruned and deleted with its generation). `EPG_INGEST_VERSION` 2, so every guide is read again once.
    - **The mappings:** `epgOverrides` in the profile (optional, by ContentKey; `epg-overrides.ts`), the `epgOverride` edit (a guide's channel, or no guide kept as `{ epgSrc: '', channel: null }`; `pin: null` takes it away); removing a user's guide drops the mappings into it. A mapping stays when a newer guide drops its channel: the channel is then without a guide.
    - **Decided (not a gate): the EPG worker reads the mappings from the profile itself** at each refresh (`readEpgOverrides`), instead of the app sending them, so every path that starts a refresh honours them (the scheduler, `refreshNow`, onboarding); a job's own `overrides` still win (tests). Their key (`epgMappingKey`) is in the basis and stored per guide (`EpgMeta.mapping`); `epgStatus.mapping` gives the profile's now, and `epgRefreshDue` says `mapping` for a guide matched with another key (after a failure's hold, before every other reason), so the next due pass or `refreshNow` reads every guide again.
    - **The search:** `QueryService.guideChannels({ epgSrc?, query, offset, limit })` and `DataEngine.guideChannels`: the active guides' declared channels in priority order, each word of the query (normalized as the matcher does) in a name or the id, pages of up to 500, a version per reload; one read per guide per reload; a guide read before 24d lists its channels in use. A channel mapped to no guide gets no panel short EPG either.

- [x] 25d. **The catch-up player: position, pause, seeking and the end.**
  - **The info bar:** the progress through the programme (elapsed of its length) and the time it aired at the current position.
  - **Pause and play:** OK, PLAY and PAUSE.
  - **Seeking:** LEFT and RIGHT (and FF/REW) in steps (longer when a key is held).
    - Inside the stream when the engine can seek it (an HLS archive playlist).
    - Otherwise by a new URL from the new start (a TS timeshift, `shift`, Xtream in whole minutes), keeping the position.
    - Seeking forward past now goes live.
  - **The programme's end:** play on into the next programme where the stream does (`timeshift_abs`, `shift`). Otherwise show a card, "The programme has ended", with "Watch live" and "Back to the guide".

    - **`e2e:media`:** the picture moves on after a seek.
    - Plus the common items above.
  - Done in leg 63 (architecture.md §1.2 "Built in step 25d"; ADR-010 "Applied in step 25d"):
    - **The controller:** `positionMs()` (the media time while a picture shows) and `seek(ms)` inside a VOD or catch-up stream on show (a reopen starts there; a pause stays a pause).
    - **The seek bar:** a catch-up's info bar has a focusable row (zone `seek`, above the buttons; UP/DOWN between them, CH± still zap): "Paused", the time from the start and the length around a bar (left to right in every language), "Aired at 21:12". OK on it (or with the bar hidden) and PLAY/PAUSE pause and play on.
    - **Seeking:** LEFT/RIGHT a minute, FF/RW five (five and ten from a held key's ninth press); one seek 600 ms after the last key. Inside an HLS archive on show; otherwise a new URL from the target rounded to the minute. Within the programme (start to last minute); forward past now goes live.
    - **The end:** a stream that plays on (`shift`, `timeshift_abs`) moves the bar to the programme aired then (5 s past the end, through `DataEngine.nowNext`); one that ends shows "The programme has ended" with "Watch live" and "Back to the guide".
    - **Decided (not a gate):** whole-minute steps (Xtream starts on minutes, and a URL seek costs a reconnect); a URL seek rounds rather than seeking inside a TS download; PLAY/PAUSE toggles (webOS maps both keys to one); RIGHT is forward in Arabic too (the media timeline doesn't mirror; the guide's does).
    - **Tests:** unit +20 (controller 5, catchup 2, player 13; 4,139 in the last full local run before the mutation fixes added 3); e2e `guide-catchup-seek.spec.ts` (en-US, ar-SA: three RIGHTs ask the mock for the start three minutes on, FF five more, LEFT stops at the start, the timeline left to right in both, DOWN/UP); `player-media.spec.ts` (`e2e:media`: a seek inside the archive's HLS with no new URL, the picture moving on; PLAY/PAUSE). ### Phase 7: Movies, series, search
- [x] 26. **Movies.** Poster grids with tiered image caps, details, resume, "Recently added".
  - **Done** with 26a-26e (legs 64-70, PRs #64-#74): the poster grid plays a movie from the mock in e2e, resume lands within 5 s of where it was left (`e2e:media`), and "Recently added" lists the newest movies first.
  - **Done when** (no Done-when of its own in leg 0; phase 7's gate is PLAN.md's "resume accurate to ±5 s"): a movie found in the poster grid plays from the mock in e2e, resumes within 5 s of where it stopped, and "Recently added" lists the newest movies first.
  - **Already built** (checked in leg 64):
    - **core:** movies are `CatalogItem`s of kind `movie`, from the Xtream path (`/movie/`) or the file extension in an M3U, and from `get_vod_streams` (`decodeListItem`: the poster in `logo`, from `stream_icon`, else `cover`; the URL with `container_extension`). `XtreamListItem` also has `added`, `rating` and `year`, but the ingest keeps only `.item`: nothing stores them. `decodeVodInfo` reads `get_vod_info` (plot, genre, cast, director, release date, duration, rating, cover, backdrops, trailer, container) and `XtreamClient.vodInfo` asks it, but nothing in the data layer or the app calls it.
    - **data:** each kind is stored in chunks of up to 500 per category, with its categories record and search segment (`putCatalogKind`); the query worker's `categories(src, kind)` and `chunk(src, kind, catIdx, chunk)` read any kind (`DataEngine.categories`/`chunk`). The profile has no VOD progress (architecture.md §1.3 plans "VOD and series progress (latest 500)").
    - **The app:** live only. The live screen's header has "Guide" (0) and "Settings" (1). `ChannelIndex` maps rows to chunks for any kind. The player plays live rows and catch-up (`mode: 'live' | 'catchup'`); its seek bar, pause and seeking (25d) are catch-up's.
    - **The player package:** `PlayRequest.mode: 'vod'`, `startMs`, the watchdog's 20 s VOD rule, a reopen at the last position, `positionMs()` and `seek(ms)` (25d). `e2e/playback-media.spec.ts` plays a VOD from 20 s through the controller, not through the app.
    - **ui:** `Tile` (a title, a subtitle, an image, a badge, a progress bar; 264×148, a landscape tile), the `grid` focus zone (`columns`, `count`, `visibleRows`), `VirtualList` (a list, not a grid). CSS Grid is missing on Chromium 53 and 56 (CLAUDE.md's cheat sheet): rows of tiles with margins.
    - **The mock:** six movies in two VOD categories (201–206: the four Blender films, the 4K HEVC pattern and the multi-audio file), `added` days ago, rating, year, `get_vod_info`, posters at `/images/movie-<id>.png` (a 1×1 PNG), and the M3U's movie lines. `?scale=` makes 120k VOD (step 4).
  - Split in leg 64 into 26a–26e, in this order:
    - **26a**, the movies screen and its poster grid, first: everything else opens from it.
    - **26b**, the grid at scale and on the old engines, next, while the grid is fresh: it is the first screen of images, and Chromium 53/56 lack CSS Grid.
    - **26c**, the details and playing a movie: the screen that resume and "Recently added" build on.
    - **26d**, resume, after playing.
    - **26e**, "Recently added", last: it needs a stored field and a query of its own.

  - **What failed:** on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, both bundles, both languages, the step "ArrowRight three programmes on" (ArrowLeft in Arabic) expected "the cursor at 01:00" and saw "the cursor at 00:30", with the view from 23:00. The movies steps after it didn't run.
  - **Find out** whether it is the fixture (no programmes past midnight?), the expectation, or the app (a real bug in the guide at midnight: then fix the app and add a unit test). Prefer pinning the walk's clock so its run doesn't depend on the hour. Reproduce it locally with a faked time (`pnpm verify:engines --engine chrome53`).
  - **Done when:** the cause is named in architecture.md §4. The walk is green at a time just before midnight in its zone, proven locally (a pinned or faked clock), and on CI (the engines job green). The PR is merged.
  - **Done in leg 67** (architecture.md §4 "Built in step 26q", runbook old-engines.md "The engine's zone"):
    - **The cause was the app.** The guide asked only the days its two-hour view crosses. CI ran at 21:15 UTC (the containers were in UTC; the "23:15" in 26c's notes was wrong). The view 21:00–23:00 left the next day unread, so the walk's three quick keys crossed midnight onto a 30-minute "No information" placeholder: 00:30, not 01:00. With the walk's keys it failed whenever the guide opened between 21:00 and 22:29 or between 00:00 and 01:59 in the engine's zone.
    - **The fix (`askedDays`):** the asks also cover the day a view after or before the view reaches, the view's own days first. Within two hours of midnight the day past it is read at the same rest.
    - **The walk's clock is pinned by zone:** each engine container starts with a `TZ` where it is 21:xx (`engineZone`; `--hour`). The harness checks the page's `getTimezoneOffset()` first. Every run now crosses midnight.
    - **The zones are digit-free names** (`FIXED_ZONES`, e.g. `Africa/Lagos`). PR #68's first CI run used `Etc/GMT+1`, and the check stopped Chromium 68 in UTC: its V8 reads the zone through ICU, which refuses a `TZ` with a digit. 53 and 56 had passed every run at 21:00 in GMT−1.
    - **Proof (Chromium 53, local):** the old build pinned to 21:48 failed exactly as CI did (en and ar). The fixed build at 21:50 passed all 8 runs; its screenshot shows the cursor on the next day's 1:00 AM programme under a 9:51 PM clock.
    - **Tests:** unit 4,332 (+6: `guide-state.test.ts` +3, one changed; `docker.test.ts` +3). Five hand mutants of `askedDays`: four caught, one equivalent. Main 374.3 KB (Tizen) / 361.6 KB (webOS), 219 core-js modules.
    - **e2e:** the full run (94 passed) failed `guide-catchup-days.spec.ts` in both languages at 23:5x local. After the player's Back the cursor was half an hour into the programme six days back: the walk back moves by 30-minute slots over days not read yet, so where it stops depends on the hour. The spec now accepts the start or half an hour in, and checks Back returns to the same cursor. It passes in both languages.
    - **Merged:** PR #68 as merge commit `72f307c`, on green CI (verify, engines; media skipped by its path filter). The engines job ran all 108 runs as expected (80 seeded failures caught) on Chromium 53, 56 and 68, each in `Atlantic/Cape_Verde` at 21:21, 21:27 and 21:33: every walk crossed midnight.

    - **The data:** profile version 5 adds `progress` (migration 4 → 5, empty), by ContentKey: the media time, the length when known, when it was saved. The latest 500 (`MAX_PROGRESS`): a new one past the cap drops the one saved longest ago, never itself. Written one movie at a time through `editProfile` `{ kind: 'progress' }`; the same place writes nothing.
    - **The saves** (the player's `progressed`): every 10 s while it plays (never opening, buffering or paused), and when it fails, ends, is left (Back, STOP, the end card, a dispose) or the app hides (the player's own `lifecycle` subscription, made before the controller's).
    - **Watched:** ended, within the last 2 minutes or past 95 % (whichever comes first), and also anything left before 10 s (it starts over): no place kept.
    - **The screens:** the details offer "Resume from 5:30" (focused), "Play from the start", "Back"; Back from the player reads the place again and focuses the first button. A tile draws its bar (`Tile.progress`) when the length is known.

      - **The app hiding:** webOS and the desktop empty the `<video>` on hide while the controller still says "playing", so a hidden app read position 0 (a save would have dropped the place; a catch-up's bar read 0 too). The player now saves first, then reads no position until it shows again.
      - **"Resume" before the details' read came back** played with no length and saved the place without one, so the tile lost its bar (the e2e caught it after a relaunch). A save with no length now keeps the known one, and the play takes it too.

    - **The data:** the ingest keeps each source's 100 newest movies by their panel's `added` (`RecentList`, newest first; equal dates in the panel's order; each kept item a copy that pins no decoded text), stored in the movie kind's categories record (`catalog_cats` `{ cats, recent }`), so it swaps and goes with its generation. No item gains a field.
    - **The query:** `QueryService.recent({ sources, kind, limit })` merges the sources' lists (one record each, no chunk read): newest first, at most 100, an equal date by the source listed first. `DataEngine.recent` calls it.

- [x] 27d. **"Continue watching".**
  - **The list:** movies and shows left part way, newest first: a movie by its place; a show by its last episode (part watched: resume it; watched: its next one, when there is one). At most 20.
  - **Where:** first in the movies rail and in the series rail (before "Recently added"/"Recently updated") when it has items; step 28's Home shows it too.
  - **Decide:** how a tile gets its item from a key (the profile keeps no item; the query worker has no lookup by key): keep a small copy of the item with the place, or a query that reads the items by key.
  - **Done when:** unit tests (the order, the cap, a watched show with and without a next episode, a removed source); e2e in English and Arabic (leave a movie and an episode part way, both in "Continue watching", OK resumes); plus the common items.
  - **Not split** (leg 75): one PR, the data first in its own commit.
  - **Decided in leg 75 (not gates):**
    - **A lookup by key in the query worker, not a copy of each item in the profile:** the loaded search segments already map every key to its chunk (`indexOfKey`, `chunkOf`), so `QueryService.itemsByKey` costs a binary search per key and one read per chunk found, with each item's own key checked. A copy would grow the profile (written every 10 s while playing) by a title, poster and URL per place, go stale, and still need the sources to drop a removed one. No profile version bump.
    - **"A next episode" is saved with the show's entry** (`ShowProgress.more`, optional, no migration): the player knows it from the details' seasons at each save; no `get_series_info` per watched show when the list is read. An entry from before is taken as the show's last. A show finished to its last episode isn't listed again when the panel adds one ("Recently updated" lists it).
    - **OK on a tile opens its details** (on "Resume from", or on the episode to resume or the next one), not the player: the details already land there, and a watched show's next episode needs its seasons.

    - **The entry is read with the categories, and again when the screen is back on top after a save** (not on every save while the player plays over it). The entry on show stays on show; in "Continue watching" the focus follows its item.

- [x] 29b1. **`crypto` and its shared test vectors.**
  - **The wrappers** (`packages/crypto`, tweetnacl 1.0.3 and @noble/hashes 2.4.0): base64url; SHA-256, HMAC-SHA256, HKDF-SHA256, scrypt; the backup keys (master, `lookupId`, `writeToken`, `encKey`, `localKey`) and envelope; the pairing box and the file's 1 MB `secretbox` frames (nonce = base + counter); Ed25519 verify against a list of keys; the blocklist's hash. Bytes in, bytes out; base64url on the wire.
  - **The vectors:** one JSON file in `packages/crypto/test/`, made by a generator that computes every value it can with Node's own crypto (OpenSSL: SHA-256, HMAC, HKDF, scrypt, X25519, Ed25519, base64url) and the rest with tweetnacl directly, never through the wrappers. No `URL` (core-js's URL polyfill is about 25 KB), no `split`, no `Array.from`, no regex built from strings; non-ASCII code points from char codes.

  - **The service** (`config-service.ts`): `ConfigService` over a `ByteTransport`, a clock and the database: `load()` (the cached config's policy, or `NO_POLICY`), `refresh({base, platform, version})` (GET `<base>/v1/config` with a 3 s limit on the whole answer and a size cap, the check, the verified copy written to `kv` under `config` with its country; on any failure the cache stays and the answer says why). Known gaps, documented: UTS #46 case-folds beyond lowercase for the Greek letters with an iota subscript, U+0345, the lunate and mathematical sigmas, U+1C80 to U+1C88 and Cherokee; some marks newer than Unicode 15. No Latin host has them. The first CI run failed on that: CI's Node 22 has older IDNA tables (U+1E9E to "ss", some ignored code points refused; Node's `domainToASCII` uses Ada's own tables, not ICU's, so the ICU version says nothing: the second run showed it), so the hand-picked answers and the ignored ranges are now pinned literals of the current UTS #46, compared with Node only when Node maps U+1E9E to the sharp s; the table's and the mapping's 21 mutants re-run against the pinned tests: 21 caught (`mutate30b1-c.log`).

    - **The player** (`refusal` option, a signal; `refused` signal): each play reads it before `controller.play` (`playRow`, so a channel, a zap, a number, PRE-CH, catch-up and the start's last channel; `playFilm`, so a movie, an episode, "Resume", "Try again" and auto-next). Refused: no play (`plays` unchanged, the last channel unchanged), the card "Playback is turned off" with the doc's message (else the catalog's) and one button, "Channels" (a channel) or "Back" (a movie), focused; the info bar stays up with it. When the signal turns on while the player is open: a movie's place is kept, the stream stops (`controller.stop()`), the track menu, a waiting zap or seek and the next episode's countdown go, and the card shows. A lift does nothing by itself (the card stays); the next play that goes through takes the card away. The start's last channel can play for a moment before the cached policy arrives (said in the docs, as for `disable-app`).

- [x] 30c3a. **The blocklist at add and on refresh, and where it shows.**
  - **The shape (leg 89, written before building):**
    - **One place checks: the data engine.** `DataEngine.refresh(job)` asks `blocked([the source's API URL])` (an Xtream account's `base`, an M3U link's `url`) before it takes the refresh slot; a blocked one rejects with `code: 'blocklisted'` and no ingest worker starts. So onboarding's add (in the loader's `checking` phase), the schedule's refresh, the hello screen's and the lab's S6 all go through it. `DataEngine.refreshEpg` asks `blocked` for each listed guide's URL: a blocked guide isn't read (left out of `only`) but stays listed, so its stored programmes aren't deleted. If `blocked` itself fails (the query worker can't read the config record), the check lets the source through with a warning: the blocklist is then unknown, and a broken worker isn't a reason to refuse a user's source.
    - **At add:** `LoadErrorCode` gains `blocklisted` (`blocked` keeps its meaning: an account the provider banned). The failed-load screen: "Couldn't add {name}", the text `error.blocklisted` ("This server can't be used with Lablo."), "Edit details" and "Try again". Nothing goes into the profile.
    - **On refresh:** the schedule's catalog refresh of a blocked source is refused by the engine, kept as a failure (tried again after `CATALOG_RETRY_MS`, so a lift is picked up within 2 h or at the next start) without the console warning; its guides on a blocked host aren't read.
    - **Where it says so:** Settings gains a second row, "Sources" (after "TV guides", so the guides screens' paths stay), opening a sources screen: each stored source in the profile's order with its name (data, isolated), its kind and host (`Xtream` / `M3U` as arguments; the host only, never a login, path or query), and one status line: "Updated {day}, {time}" (the catalog's `checkedAt`, else `updatedAt`), "Not updated yet", or for a blocked source "Not updated: this server can't be used with Lablo." (`data-blocked="true"`). It reads `profile`, `sourceStatus` and `blocked` when it opens; no actions yet (OK does nothing; Back returns to Settings). The guides screen: a guide whose URL is blocked shows "Not read: this server can't be used with Lablo." in place of its failure line (`data-blocked`).
    - **Not covered (said in the docs):** the final host after an M3U link's redirects at add and on refresh (the ingest worker follows them; 30c3b checks redirects only for streams, on Tizen); a guide link typed in onboarding is checked when it is read, not at add.

Refused: the player's refusal card (30c2b's) with its own text, and the screen's own black (`player__blank`).
  - **Decisions for its leg:** whether a host blocked while a stream plays stops it (as `disable-playback` does); how the check's wait (an async worker call) fits `playRow`'s zap timing; an e2e stream host that differs from the source's API host (the mock's URLs follow the Host header).
  - **Done when:** unit tests per play path and for the redirect probe's host; e2e with a served config blocking the stream host at play; en and ar screenshots looked at; plus the common items.
  - **The shape (leg 90, written before building):**
    - **One place checks: the player controller** (`packages/player`, like 30c3a's data engine). Every play of the app (a channel, a zap, a number, PRE-CH, catch-up and its seeks by URL, the start's last channel, a movie, an episode, "Resume", "Try again", auto-next) and every retry goes through `PlayerController.open`, so `ControllerOptions.blocked(urls)` is asked there: the URL about to open, **alongside the engine's close** (close-before-open already waits for it, so a zap waits only when the worker answers slower than the close); on Tizen, after the redirect probe, the probe's answer too when it differs. A blocked URL: no `engine.load`, no retry rung (the other URLs of a request share the host), no account or connectivity check; the controller gives up at once with the failure `{category: 'blocked', code: 'CFG-BLOCKLIST'}`. A rejected or failed check blocks nothing (the blocklist is unknown: `DataEngine.blockedOrNone`, made public, warns). `seq`/token checks drop an answer that lands after a zap, a stop or a hide.
    - **A host blocked while a stream plays stops it** (decided: a block is a kill for that host, and "a kill reaches the TV within one config refresh"): `PlayerController.checkBlocklist()` asks again about the URL on show (and the probe's answer), and stops it as at open; player-state calls it whenever `config.last` changes (a `blocklist` option, a signal) while the player is open. The query worker has read the new list before `config.last` changes (30b2's `reloadConfig`).
    - **The card:** player-state turns a `blocked` failure into the refusal card (30c2b's `refuse`: the menu, a waiting seek or countdown go, a movie's place is kept, the stream stops, `player__blank` paints black) with `BLOCKLIST_REFUSAL` (`id: 'BLOCKLIST'`: no rule id has capitals, so `data-rule="BLOCKLIST"` tells it apart) and its own texts: "Can't play this" / "Its server can't be used with Lablo." (`player.blocked.*`, en and ar), one button ("Channels" or "Back"). The error card's text table gets the same texts for the new category (never shown in the app: the refusal takes over).
    - **Unlike `disable-playback`:** the check is asynchronous, so a blocked play has already counted in `plays` and, for a live channel, become the last channel (`tuned`): the next start shows the card for it (said in the docs).
    - **Not covered (said in the docs):** where `<video>` follows redirects itself (webOS, the desktop) only the URL asked for is checked; an HLS stream's variant playlists and segments on other hosts; a movie's subtitle files.

Each a PR with the common items. Nothing goes to production before step 34: migrations run on the local and test databases only (no approval needed).
- [x] 31a. **The pairing API** (`apps/web`, architecture.md §3.2 and §3.3 A).
  - **The shape (leg 91, written before building):**
    - **The routes:** `POST /v1/pair` (the TV's `tvPk` in; `{sid, code, pollToken, exp}` out), `GET /v1/pair/:code` (`{tvPk, exp}` for a typed code), `POST /v1/pair/:code/payload` (the sealed `{phonePk, nonce, ct}`, once), `POST /v1/pair/:code/upload-url` (a presigned PUT of the encrypted file's exact size, before the payload), `GET /v1/pair/s/:sid` (the TV's poll: `waiting`, or `ready` with the payload, which is deleted in the same statement), `POST /v1/pair/s/:sid/status` (once, after the read). **Two more than §3.2's table:** `GET /v1/pair/:code/status` (the phone's progress: `open`, `sent`, `received`, or `done` with the TV's status category) and `GET /v1/pair/s/:sid/file` (the TV's presigned GET for the session's file; step 31d uses it). Their schemas go into `@lablo/protocol` with samples.
    - **Secrets on the wire and at rest:** the code is 8 Crockford characters from 40 random bits, stored as b64url(SHA-256) (unique; a clash makes a new code); the poll token is 32 random bytes sent as `Authorization: Bearer`, stored as `tokenHash`; the sid is 16 random bytes. The row keeps `tvPk` (public), the sealed payload until the TV reads it, the TV's status category and two new columns (migration `pair_progress`): `deliveredAt` and `fileId` (the uploaded object's id; the blob is `pair/<sid>/<fileId>`).
    - **Time:** the code lives 10 minutes (`expiresAt`): a payload or upload after it is refused (404). The TV may read a payload posted in time, and the phone may read the progress, until the row goes: an hour after it was made. Each `POST /v1/pair` sweeps rows past that hour and their blobs; the TV's status deletes the session's blob at once.
    - **Rate limits per IP** (`rateKey`, never the IP itself): making codes 20 per 10 minutes; anything that names a code (a guess) 30 per 10 minutes, except the phone's progress poll, 400 per 10 minutes, like the TV's poll, status and file. Refused: 429 with `Retry-After`.
    - **Bodies:** read with a byte cap before parsing (413 over it: 64 KB for the payload, 1 KB for the rest), then the protocol's schema (400). Every answer is JSON with `cache-control: no-store`. The TV's routes answer any origin (`access-control-allow-origin: *`, an `OPTIONS` preflight for `authorization` and `content-type`); the phone's are same-origin only (no CORS headers).
    - **Services:** one `MemoryBlobStore` and one `MemoryRateLimiter` per process (on `globalThis`, like the database client), and a rate-key secret from `LABLO_RATE_SECRET` or a random one per process. On Vercel (`VERCEL` set) without the production adapters, they refuse (503): step 34 adds R2 and Upstash.
  - **Done-when checks (runnable):**
    - `pnpm test:web` with `apps/web/test/pair.web.test.ts` against the test database: each route's answers (made, a typed code, sent once, the upload's URL redeemable for exactly its size, the poll's `waiting` then `ready` once, the status once, the file's GET URL, the progress through all four states); expiry (a code past 10 minutes; a row past the hour swept with its blob); the poll token (missing, wrong, another session's); the rate limits (the 31st guess refused with `Retry-After`); the bodies (malformed, over the cap, schema errors); the TV's CORS and preflight; the migration and no-drift checks; **"the server only ever holds ciphertext":** a whole pairing with an Xtream login and an M3U file carrying marker strings, after every step every row of every table (as JSON text) and every blob searched for the markers, the code and the poll token: none found, and the stored payload equal to what the phone sent;
    - unit tests (in `pnpm test`): the code (alphabet, length, 40 bits used), the hashes, the body reader, the bearer and the client IP readers, the CORS headers, the services' Vercel refusal; the protocol's new schemas and samples (the vectors page runs them: `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68`);
    - plus the common items (mutation with the list written first, `pnpm verify` parts, the PR merged on green CI with its `web` job, the docs: architecture.md "Built in step 31a", runbook `web.md`).
  - **Done in leg 91** (architecture.md §3.2 "Built in step 31a", runbook `web.md` "Pairing"):
    - **Built as planned, with these choices:** the payload's read, the status and an upload are each one `UPDATE … FROM (SELECT … FOR UPDATE)` returning the old value (two polls at once: one gets it, the other 409); the poll answers 410 when the code ended with nothing sent; the file's GET URL only after the read (409 before); the code is 5 random bytes read 5 bits at a time; routes are thin wrappers over `pair.ts` (`pairRoute`: 503 with the TV's CORS when the database or services fail, the log only the operation and the error's kind); `.env.example` names the two optional variables.

    - **Files:** `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` (the first page, so the first root layout: `<html lang dir>`, no index); `src/app/p/page.tsx` (a server component: the language from `?lang=en|ar`, else `Accept-Language`, else English; it renders the client page); `src/phone/` (browser code only): `PhonePage.tsx` (`'use client'`), `state.ts` (the page's state as a pure reducer), `flows.ts` (the requests behind each action, with the API injected), `progress.ts` (the 2 s progress poll, with the clock injected), `code.ts` (the typed code and the QR fragment), `form.ts` (the fields, the links, the payload), `api.ts` (the page's three requests), `catalog.ts` (en and ar), `phone.module.css`.
    - **The language:** the page's own small catalog (`apps/web` may import only `protocol` and `crypto`), its terms the TV's (`field.*`, `error.*`); a test holds both languages to the same keys and placeholders. A toggle at the top switches in place (the typed values stay; `?lang` follows with `history.replaceState`). `Xtream`, `M3U`, the code and links are isolated (`<bdi>`, `dir="ltr"` inputs). The layout is one flex column with logical properties (`margin-inline-start`, `text-align: start`): every phone browser Next 16 supports (Chrome 111, Safari 16.4) has them, so no rtlcss pipeline. System fonts: the page loads nothing from another host.
    - **The code:** typed as people read it: upper-cased, I and L read as 1, O as 0, spaces and dashes dropped, then the protocol's `pairCode` (8 Crockford characters, U refused). The QR link's fragment `#c=<code>&k=<tvPk>` is read once on load (the code by the same rule, the key as `bytes32`) and then removed from the address bar (`history.replaceState`). The QR path uses the fragment's key, never the server's, and first reads the code's progress (`open` → the form; `sent`, `received`, `done` → the progress; 404 → "this code has ended"); the typed path gets the key from `GET /v1/pair/:code` (404 → no TV shows it; 429 → wait, with the minutes from `Retry-After`).
    - **The forms:** Xtream (server, username, password) or M3U link (playlist link); then up to four guide links (one field, "Add another", each removable) and an optional name. Links follow the TV form's `normalizeLink` (trimmed, `http://` added when no scheme was typed, http(s) only, `host[:port]` with no login; a root test holds the two functions to the same answers on samples, since neither app may import the other); logins trimmed; empty guide fields dropped; an empty name left out (the TV names a source by its host). The result is parsed by `pairPayloadSchema` before sealing. Problems per field in the TV's words (`field.required`, `field.url`, `field.spaces`).
    - **Sending:** `sealPairPayload(utf8(JSON.stringify(payload)), tvPk)` in the browser, then `POST …/payload`: `sent` → the progress; 409 → the progress (it was already sent); 404 → the code ended: the page asks for a new code (typed, or scan again) and keeps the form's values. After a `done` that failed, "Fix and send again" goes back to the code with the values kept. "Your details are encrypted on this phone; only your TV can read them."
    - **The progress:** `GET …/status` every 2 s while the progress shows: sent → "Sent. Waiting for your TV", received → "Your TV has it. Checking the source", `done` → the TV's category in the TV's words (`ok`: added; `auth_failed`, `expired`, `unreachable`, `blocked`); stops at `done` or 404 (the row is gone after an hour: "check your TV"); a failed read waits for the next tick. `aria-live` on the progress.
    - **Only the page's own origin:** `api.ts` is the one `apps/web` file allowed `fetch` (a narrow lint exception for relative `/v1/pair/` paths, proven in `test/lint-web-outbound.test.ts`: `fetch` still refused in every other `apps/web` file, `XMLHttpRequest` and the rest still refused in it); answers checked with the protocol's schemas. A `Content-Security-Policy` on `/p` in production builds (`connect-src 'self'`, `default-src 'self'`, `form-action 'self'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `object-src 'none'`; `'unsafe-inline'` scripts and styles for Next's inline bootstrap: a nonce needs a proxy and buys little on a page that renders nobody's HTML) and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; `next dev` gets none (its reloads need eval and a websocket).
    - **E2E:** `playwright.web.config.ts` (`pnpm e2e:web`, after `pnpm build:web`): `next start` on port 3101 against a fresh `lablo_e2e` database (global setup: drop, create, migrate), the specs in `apps/web/e2e/`, a phone viewport. The test plays the TV in Node (`@lablo/crypto`, the API over HTTP). Each test sends its own `x-forwarded-for` (Next's own server keeps a client's; Vercel's edge sets it), so the rate limits don't meet across tests. CI's `web` job installs Chromium and runs it after `build:web`.

  - **Decisions (leg 93):**
    - **The QR encoder is our own** (`packages/core/src/qr.ts`), not a dependency. Weighed: `uqr` 0.1.3 (Nayuki's encoder ported, MIT, no dependencies) or `qrcode-generator` 2.0.4 against about 250 lines of our own. Every mode and option of a library comes with it, and Babel adds a core-js module for every API name a file mentions (typed arrays, `Array.from`, `fill`, `map`), measured per file before tree-shaking; our own is byte mode only, one error-correction level, plain number arrays and `for` loops. Its correctness comes from two oracles, both devDependencies of `packages/core` only (never shipped): `uqr` gives the exact modules for the same text, version and mask, and `jsqr` 1.4.0 (an independent decoder) reads every matrix back.
    - **The pairing client runs in a worker of its own** (`packages/data/src/pair.worker.ts`, started when the screen opens and ended when it closes), like the config worker: the network, the X25519 key, `tweetnacl`'s box and the zod schemas stay out of main.js (439.5 KB of 500 now; step 29b2a measured the schemas alone at about 35 KB). The secret key never leaves the worker. The QR's modules are made there too, so main only draws them.
    - **The QR link** is `<api base>/p#c=<code>&k=<tvPk>`: the phone page and the API are one Next app on one origin, so the build's `__API_BASE__` (or a test's `?api=`) is both. The screen shows the same address without its scheme for typing (`127.0.0.1:3100/p` in development).
- [x] 31c1. **The QR encoder and the pairing client** (`packages/core`, `packages/data`, the data engine).
  - **The shape (leg 93, written before building):**
    - **`packages/core/src/qr.ts`:** `encodeQr(text)` → `{ size, rows }` (`rows[y]` a string of `1` dark and `0` light, no quiet zone: the screen draws it) or `null` when the text doesn't fit. UTF-8 bytes (by hand: core has no `TextEncoder`), byte mode, error correction M (15%: a TV screen photographed at an angle), the smallest version from 1 to 10 that holds it (a production link is about 80 bytes: version 5 or 6), the mask with the lowest penalty (ISO/IEC 18004's four rules). `checkQr()` (its own entry `@lablo/core/check-qr`): golden matrices made in Node, as a hash per case, so the vectors page proves the encoder on Chromium 53, 56 and 68.
    - **`packages/data/src/pair-service.ts`:** `PairService` over an injected `fetch` and clock. `open(base)`: a fresh key pair (`newBoxKeyPair`), `POST <base>/v1/pair {tvPk}`, the answer through `pairCreateResponseSchema`; keeps the sid, the poll token and the secret key; answers `{ code, exp, link, typed, qr }` (`typed`: the address without its scheme). `poll()`: `GET <base>/v1/pair/s/<sid>` with `Authorization: Bearer <pollToken>`: `waiting`; `ready` with the payload opened (`openPairPayload`), parsed (`JSON.parse`, `pairPayloadSchema`) and the key forgotten; `unreadable` (it didn't open or parse: the key forgotten too); `ended` (410: the code ended with nothing sent; 404: the row is gone; 401); `wait` with the milliseconds from `Retry-After` (429); `failed` (the network, a time limit, 5xx, a malformed answer). `status(category)`: `POST <base>/v1/pair/s/<sid>/status` once (`ok` or `failed`). `close()` forgets everything. Bodies are written by hand (no `JSON.stringify` in TV code), answers read with a byte cap and a 10 s limit (Chromium 53 can't abort a fetch: a late answer is dropped), errors never hold a URL, the code or a token.
    - **`pair.worker.ts`:** the RPC handlers (`open`, `poll`, `status`, `close`) over one `PairService` with the worker's `fetch`. **`DataEngine.pairing()`:** starts a pair worker and gives its client; `close()` ends the worker. `startPairWorker` joins the engine's factories (`workers.ts`), and the worker's npm deps go into Vite's `optimizeDeps.include` if it lacks them.
  - **Done-when checks (runnable):**
    - unit (`pnpm test`): `qr.test.ts`: the version table (data and EC codewords, blocks), the Reed-Solomon remainders, the format and version bits, then the whole matrix equal to `uqr`'s for the same text, version and mask (all eight masks, versions 1 to 10, ASCII and Arabic text), the chosen mask equal to `uqr`'s choice, every matrix read back by `jsqr`, the too-long text; `pair-service.test.ts` with a scripted phone (`sealPairPayload`) and a fake server: each answer of each route, the bearer, the bodies, the time limit, the byte cap, a payload sealed to another key, bad JSON, a schema failure, the key forgotten after `ready`, `unreadable` and `close`; the worker over a `MessageChannel`; the engine's `pairing()` starts and ends one worker;
    - the build: the pair worker against its 150 KB budget, main.js measured before and after (no more than the import's placeholder), the worker's core-js modules listed (`artifacts/babel-polys30b2.ts`);
    - the vectors page with `checkQr` in page and worker: e2e `crypto-vectors.spec.ts`, then `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68`;
    - plus the common items (mutation with the list written first, `pnpm verify` parts, the PR merged on green CI, the docs: architecture.md "Built in step 31c1").
  - **Done in leg 93** (architecture.md §3.2 "Built in step 31c1"; no runbook change: nothing runs differently yet):
    - **Built as planned, with these choices:** `qrFits` checks the link's length before the code is asked for (the code is 8 ASCII characters, so a probe link with `00000000` is as long); the QR level is raised to Q or H when the text still fits the same version (as Nayuki's encoder does); a new code forgets the session before it even when it fails; `status` keeps the session on a 429, a server error or no connection, so the screen can send it again, and ends it on 200, 409, 404 and 401; the payload is decoded as strict UTF-8; `retryAfterMs` takes whole seconds only (no `trim()`: `Headers` trims, and `trim` brings a core-js module); the timings live in `pair-constants.ts` so `@lablo/data/client` can give them to the screen without the service; `engine.pairing()` needs no close guard (both calls repeat safely).

- [x] 31d. **The M3U file relay.**
  - The phone's file form: the file read in 1 MiB frames, each sealed (`sealFileFrame`) and uploaded through the presigned PUT; `fileKey`, the nonce base and the size in the boxed payload. In development and e2e a `/dev/blob/<key>` route answers the memory store's URLs (never on Vercel). The TV: the presigned GET, `openFileFrame` frame by frame into the M3U ingest (no whole-file copy), a frame that fails to open ends the add; an IndexedDB store only if the ingest needs one (then a database version bump with an upgrade test from version 2). Decide how a development URL (http) passes the upload schema's https rule.
  - **Done when:** unit tests (frames, a cut, swapped or changed frame refused); e2e: a multi-frame file sent from the phone page, ingested on the TV, the blob gone after the status; plus the common items.
  - **Split in leg 97** (too large for one leg) into 31d1 (the file's way up and down: the protocol's URL rule, the `/dev/blob` route, the phone page's file form) and 31d2 (the TV: the file fetched and opened frame by frame into the M3U ingest, a source with no link). 31d is ticked when both are.
  - **Decided in leg 97: development URLs are relative paths (option b).** The memory store presigns `/dev/blob/<key>?m=…&n=…&e=…&s=…`, which the phone page and the TV resolve against the API's origin. Why: the page, the API and the development blobs are then one origin, so a phone on the LAN needs no configuration, the page's `connect-src 'self'` holds with no development exception, and 31e's test front sees the file's bytes pass like every other request. The upload and file answers' schema takes an `https://` URL (R2) or exactly that path shape (`/dev/blob/`, the blob key's characters, a `?` and the query's: no `//`, no dots); a relative path can only name the API's own origin, and the route answers 404 on Vercel, so production opens nothing. `LABLO_DEV_BLOB_URL` goes. Option (a) (http for loopback hosts only) was turned down: absolute `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` URLs need setting per machine and port, a LAN phone can't use them, and a page served through 31e's front would meet them as another origin (its CSP refuses those).
- [x] 31d1. **The file's way up and down** (`@lablo/protocol`, `apps/web`: the blob route and the phone page).
  - **The shape (leg 97, written before building):**
    - **The protocol:** `presignedFile.url` is `^https://\S+$` or `^/dev/blob/<key>?<query>$` (key characters `[A-Za-z0-9_/-]`, query characters `[A-Za-z0-9_=&-]`); samples for the path, a protocol-relative `//host`, a dotted path, `http://` (still refused).
    - **`MemoryBlobStore`:** its base is `/dev/blob` (relative); `redeem(method, pathAndQuery, body)` as before. `services.ts` drops `LABLO_DEV_BLOB_URL` and `DEV_BLOB_URL`.
    - **The route `src/app/dev/blob/[...key]/route.ts`:** `PUT` and `GET` through `redeem` with the request's path and query; the PUT's body read with a cap (the URL's `n`, at most `PAIR_FILE_MAX_BYTES`: past it 400, as a wrong length); the GET answers `application/octet-stream` with `access-control-allow-origin: *` (the TV is another origin, as R2's CORS rule will allow); every answer `cache-control: no-store`; R2's codes (403 a bad, expired or other-method URL; 400 a wrong length; 404 nothing there); 404 on Vercel (`VERCEL` set) and whenever the services' store isn't the memory one.
    - **The phone's file form:** a third source type, "M3U file" (`file`): a file input (no `accept` filter: iOS greys out extensions it doesn't know); the chosen file in the state as `{name, size}`, its handle beside it; its problems: none chosen (`required`), empty (`empty`), too large (`large`: its encrypted length over `PAIR_FILE_MAX_BYTES`, so at most 41,942,400 plain bytes). The guide links and the name as for the other types; an empty name becomes the file's name without `.m3u`/`.m3u8` (cut to 80), so the TV can show something better than "a file".
    - **Sending a file** (`flows.ts`): `uploadUrl(code, fileCipherLength(size))` first (404: the code ended, the form and the file kept; 429: limited); then the file read one 1 MiB frame at a time (`slice(…).arrayBuffer()`), each `sealFileFrame`d under a fresh `fileKey` and nonce base, the frames joined into one `Blob`; then `upload(url, blob)` (a PUT of exactly that length; anything but 200 is a network error, and sending again asks for a new upload URL, which replaces the file); then the payload `{v: 1, source: {type: 'm3u-file', file: {key, fileKey, base, size}}, name?, epg?}` through `pairPayloadSchema`, sealed and posted as now. A file that can't be read: "This file couldn't be read" on the field. The button says "Encrypting and sending the file…" while it runs.
    - **`api.ts`:** `uploadUrl(code, size)` (`POST /v1/pair/:code/upload-url`, `pairUploadResponseSchema`) and `upload(url, body)` (`PUT`, `credentials: 'omit'`, `redirect: 'error'`, no referrer; only a URL the schema passed). **For step 34:** R2's host joins the page's `connect-src`, and the R2 bucket's CORS rule allows a PUT from the page's origin and a GET from any.

    - unit (`pnpm test`): the samples; the blob store's relative URLs; the route (PUT then GET, 403, 400 short and long, 404, a second PUT replacing, Vercel, CORS, no-store); the form (each file problem, the payload with the file, the default name); the reducer (the file picked, kept by "Fix and send again", cleared by "Add another source"); the flows with a fake API (the upload before the payload, the frames opening with the payload's key to the file, each failure's view); `api.ts`'s two calls (paths, statuses, a URL the schema refuses never fetched); the catalogs;

    - plus the common items (mutation with the list written first, `pnpm verify` parts, `test:web`, `build:web`, `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68` for the samples on the vectors page, the PR merged on green CI with its `web` job, the docs: architecture.md "Built in step 31d1", runbook `web.md`).
  - **Built in leg 97** (architecture.md §3.2 "Built in step 31d1", runbook `web.md` "The M3U file"): as the shape says, with these choices: the frames are sealed before the upload URL is asked for (a file that can't be read costs no code guess, and the URL's 10 minutes start later); a file that can't be read, or has changed size since it was chosen, is forgotten with "This file couldn't be read. - [x] 31d2a. **The file's bytes on the TV** (`packages/data`, `apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts`).
  - **The shape (leg 98, written before building):**
    - **`file-frames.ts`, `openFileFrames(source, file)`:** a `ByteSource` of the plain file over the download's `ByteSource` and the payload's `{fileKey, base, size}`. Frame i's exact ciphertext (`FILE_FRAME_BYTES` + 16, the last one shorter) is gathered across network chunks and opened with `openFileFrame(…, i)`; one frame is held at a time. A frame that doesn't open, a download that ends early, and one byte past `fileCipherLength(size)` each fail with `FileError` (code `file`), the last checked by one more read after the last frame; the connection's own failures pass through (`NetworkError`); `cancel` reaches the download.
    - **`PairService.file(port)`** (`pairHandlers.file`): only after `ready` with an `m3u-file` payload (the session keeps the payload's `file`; the payload still goes to the app as now). `GET <base>/v1/pair/s/<sid>/file` with the poll token, in `PAIR_TIMEOUT_MS`: 200 through `pairFileUrlResponseSchema`, whose `key` must be the payload's; 404 is `gone` (no file came), 429 `rate`, other statuses `status`. The URL resolved (a path against the base, `https://` as is), then its GET (no token), headers in `PAIR_TIMEOUT_MS`: 200 with no `content-length` or exactly `fileCipherLength(size)`; 403 or 404 (expired or deleted) and another length are `file`. Then `read` and `cancel` are served on the port over `openFileFrames` (each frame's buffer transferred). Answers `{ok: true, size}` or `{ok: false, failure}` (`network`, `timeout`, `rate`, `status`, `malformed`, `gone`, `file`). A second `file` call, a new code, the status and `close` end the feed before them. Results and errors never hold a URL.
    - **`file-feed.ts`, `feedResponse(port, size)`:** the ingest worker's side, a `ByteResponse` (status 200, `content-length` the plain size, for the progress bar) reading the port by RPC; a remote `file` error becomes `IngestError('file')`, any other remote failure `NetworkError`; `cancel` tells the pair worker and lets the port go.
    - **The ingest:** `IngestSource` gains `{type: 'm3u-file'}`; `IngestRunRequest.file` (`{port, size}`, transferred with the call); `runIngest`'s `m3u-file` branch reads `options.file()` through the M3U parser exactly as a link's body (no validators, no panel login; the byte hash still makes the same file `unchanged`), and fails with `IngestError('file')` without a feed. `IngestErrorCode` gains `file`.
    - **`engine.ts`:** `PairSession.file()` makes the `MessageChannel`, transfers one port to the pair worker's `file` and answers `{ok: true, feed: {port, size}}` with the other (closed on a failure); `RefreshOptions.file` transfers the feed with `run` (a feed serves one refresh); `sourceUrlOf` answers `null` for a file and `refresh` then checks no catalog server (a file has none; its streams meet the blocklist in `PlayerController.play`).
    - **Ending order:** a load is cancelled before its pair worker ends (pair-state's `close` already runs `cancelLoad` before `endSession`): an ingest reading a feed whose worker is gone would wait until it is cancelled. Written in both modules' comments.
  - **Done-when checks (runnable):**
    - unit (`pnpm test`): `file-frames.test.ts` (fast-check chunk splits with empty chunks open to exactly the file; a cut at a frame's edge and inside one, two frames swapped, a changed byte in a frame's text and in its tag, one byte more, another file key, the size one off either way: each `file`; a network error passes through; cancel reaches the download; no read returns more than a frame); `pair-service.test.ts` (`file`: the two requests, a path URL and an https one, the token on the first only, each status, the key and length checks, a payload without a file, after `close`, a second call; the frames over a real `MessageChannel`); `file-feed.test.ts` (reads, the error mapping, cancel); `ingest.test.ts` (a three-frame file to a catalog, its header's guides kept; a broken frame: `file` and no generation left; the same file again: `unchanged`; no feed: `file`); `test/tv-data-engine.test.ts` (a scripted phone's file: `pairing().file()`, then `refresh` with the feed fills the catalog, both workers over message channels; a broken file fails the refresh with `file`);
