  - **Decided in leg 99 (the leg's calls, as leg 98 left them):**
    - **The feed reaches the loader through the draft.** `SourceDraft` gains `{type: 'm3u-file', name, epgUrls, file}`, where `file()` resolves a `FileFeed` or rejects with a `LoadError` (`gone` and `file` are `file`, every other `PairFileFailure` `network`). Pair-state builds it from the session the payload came on. Why: the loader stays the one add path (the slot, the refresh, the guides, the profile), and the onboarding form never makes a file draft.
    - **The feed is asked for between two slot waits:** the slot free, then `file()` (two requests, up to 20 s), then the slot free again (normally no wait) and the refresh at once. Why: the refresh must claim the slot with nothing awaited in between, and a feed asked for before a long wait would hold the blob's connection idle. A feed that a cancel leaves unused is let go by the screen's close (the pair worker ends) or the status (the pair service ends it).
    - **Profile version 8:** `SourceRecord` gains `{id, type: 'm3u-file', name, added}`; the 7 → 8 migration changes only `v`. Why: an older app would read a file source as a link with no URL; a newer document is one it never saves over. Older versions' types keep the two-variant `SourceRecordV7`.
    - **Sent again under the same name replaces:** a file whose name is a stored file source's (exact, after trimming) loads under that source's id and keeps its `added`, so favourites, places and the rail's choices carry over (the `source` edit replaces in place); another name adds a source. Its guide links the list doesn't have yet get `g<n>` ids (`nextGuideId`, moved from the guides screen to the loader, over the listed ids and the stored guides' ids), never `<id>`, `<id>-2`…: those may name a guide the user removed whose records are still stored.
    - **A failed or refused file offers only "Show a new code":** the status already went, and the server deletes the blob with it, so "Try again" could only fail.
    - **A file with no name** (the protocol allows it; the phone page always sends the file's) gets the catalog's `pair.fileName` ("Playlist from your phone"), given to `pairDraft` by pair-state.
    - **Settings › Sources** shows a file source as "{format} file" with no server, never asks the blocklist about it, and words its line "Sent from your phone {day}, {time}" (it is never updated by itself).

  - **Decided in leg 100 (the leg's calls, as leg 99 left them):**
    - **The stand-in's blob uses development's URL shape:** `/dev/blob/pair/<sid>/<fileId>?m=PUT|GET&n=<size>&e=<exp>&s=<token>`, a path on the stand-in's own origin, which the TV resolves against its API base exactly as it does against `next start`. The blobs are a map in memory. A URL is honoured only exactly as the stand-in gave it, before `e` (another: 403): a PUT of exactly `n` bytes (else 400), a GET answering the bytes with CORS `*` and their `content-length`, 404 once the blob is gone. The tokens are random and kept in a map (no HMAC: the same answers for a test, less code). Why: the protocol's schema takes only `https://` or that path, so a simpler shape would be refused by the TV, and the TV's code must meet the same resolution it meets in development.
    - **The old-engine walk:** the English walk keeps the playlist's link (31c2b's proof), and the Arabic walk sends the fixture's playlist as a file, padded at its end with `#PAD` comment lines to three frames. So every engine and platform proves both ways in about the time the walk takes now, and the file crosses the `MessageChannel` frame by frame. The Arabic walk's profile wants `s1,m3u-file,<name>`, and the stand-in must hold no blob after the status.
    - **A broken file, as the scripted phone makes it:** one bit flipped in the last frame's ciphertext after sealing, so the first frames open and reach the ingest worker before the last fails its check. That proves a half-read file keeps nothing.
    - **The file's frames are copied over the `MessageChannel`, never transferred** (found by 31d2b2a's old-engine walk): on Chromium 53 a message posted on a `MessagePort` with an `ArrayBuffer` in its transfer list arrives as `null`, so the ingest worker never got a frame. A page with two workers on one channel showed it on 53, and showed 56 and 68 delivering the transfer. A 1 MiB copy costs a few milliseconds; an engine check would cost more code than it saves. Why it slipped through: Node's `MessageChannel` and Chromium 56+ deliver the transfer, and no old engine had run the file path until now.
    - **Arabic `done.body` becomes «تمت إضافة {name}.»** ("{name} was added"; decided while building 31d2b2a, from its screenshots). The old «{name} جاهز.» is masculine and read wrong after the file's feminine default name «قائمة من هاتفك», on the pairing screen's done view and onboarding's done screen. The passive reads right whatever the name, and the English stays "{name} is ready.". This was on the handover's list of later fixes; step 46's native review can still reword it.

- [x] 32. **Backup.** Encrypted backup and automatic restore (scrypt master key, envelope), backup-code fallback. Ticked in leg 115 with 32e (32a to 32e merged).
  - **Done when:** a simulated webOS wipe restores automatically.
  - **Split in leg 102** (far too large for one leg) into 32a (the API in `apps/web`), 32b (the TV's device secret, master key and derived keys), 32c (the envelope's content, the upload schedule and the restore at startup), 32d (Settings, the backup code's screens, the wipe e2e and the old-engine walk: step 32's own "Done when") and 32e (logins at rest under `localKey`). 32 is ticked when all five are.
  - **Decided in leg 102 (each with its why; a later leg may refine one where building shows more, and writes that down):**
    - **The envelope passes through the API and lives in the blob store** at `backup/<lookupId>`; the `Backup` row keeps only the lookup ID, the write token's hash, the size and the dates. Why: 512 KB fits a Vercel function's body with room to spare, so no presigned round trip is needed (pairing's files need one only because they reach 40 MB), and §3.2 already says "the envelope itself is in R2".
    - **`GET` needs no token and reads only the blob store.** Why: the lookup ID is itself a 256-bit secret derived from the master key, and the envelope is ciphertext to anyone without that key; and a restore right after a wipe is the one moment the backup exists for, so it shouldn't also depend on Postgres being up.
    - **The first `PUT` claims a lookup ID for its write token** (the row keeps `tokenHash(writeToken)`); every later `PUT` and every `DELETE` needs the same token (401 otherwise). The row is written before the blob, so a caller with the wrong token never changes the stored envelope.
    - **No migration.** The `Backup` table from step 29a's `init` migration already has every column 32a needs.
    - **Retention is the API's own sweep** (rows 180 days without an update, and their blobs, a bounded number at each `PUT`); step 34 may add R2's lifecycle rule as a second line.
    - **A phone-sent file is not in the backup.** Step 31d2b1 stores a file source with no bytes (`{id, type: 'm3u-file', name, added}`), and the envelope's cap is 512 KB. A restored file source comes back by name, with its favourites and places kept under its id, and asks to be sent again from the phone (sending a file under a stored file source's name already replaces it under the same id). architecture.md's storage table row ("IDB `blobs` … included in the backup if ≤ 5 MB") is corrected in 32c. Why not keep the bytes: a profile and IndexedDB change, 30 MB more on TVs with little storage, and a second upload path, for a file the user still has on the phone.
    - **The envelope's plaintext is the gzipped profile document, whole** (sources, guides, favourites, places, settings): everything else on the TV is derived and re-ingested. With "include login details" off, each source goes without its login or link (name, type and id kept), and after a restore asks for them again. 32c fixes the exact fields.
    - **The backup code** (the fallback when there is no device ID: "Not now" at the consent screen, or a platform that gives none): 128 random bits made on the TV at first need, kept in IndexedDB, shown in Settings as 26 Crockford base32 characters in groups, for the user to photograph. After a wipe such a TV has nothing to derive from, so the first-run screens offer "Restore with a backup code" and the user types it. 32d builds both screens.
    - **Logins at rest under `localKey` come last (32e)**, after the restore is proved. Why: it changes how every source is stored and read (a profile version 9 with its pins, and the master key needed before any URL is built), and step 32's "Done when" doesn't depend on it. Its limit is stated as §3.3 B states it: it protects a copied IndexedDB, not someone holding the TV's device ID too; with a backup code (kept in the same IndexedDB) it protects nothing, and 32e says so rather than pretend.
  - **Decided by Omar (2026-11-18, in leg 103's tab): (a), look for a backup first.** What it means for 32c (its shape settles the rest):
    - At a start with no profile, before onboarding asks anything, the TV reads its device ID (no consent asked yet: this one read is what the decision allows), derives the keys in the backup worker and asks `GET /v1/backup/:lookupId` once. Found and opened: the profile comes back (its own `consent: true` with it), "Welcome back", and the catalogs re-ingest in the background. Not found, unreadable, or no device ID: nothing derived is kept (the worker's `kv` master record goes), and onboarding runs as now.
    - 32b's `backupRequest` reads the ID only when `consent === true`: 32c adds this probe as its own, separately named path (so the "only after Allow" rule stays true everywhere else), with a test that it runs only at a start with no profile.
    - The consent screen's text and architecture.md's privacy text say the ID is used "to find and encrypt your backup" (en and ar catalogs); the first-run list in architecture.md (item 3) says the ID is read once at a first start to look for a backup.
    - Left for 32c's shape: whether the start waits for the look-up (scrypt is 1 s on Chromium 53, 3.4 s on 56, perhaps 10+ s on a 2018 TV) behind a "Looking for your backup" screen with progress, or shows onboarding at once and switches to "Welcome back" when a backup is found; and a time limit on the look-up.
    - A TV with no device ID (a backup code) has nothing to look for: 32d's "Restore with a backup code" screen covers it.
  - **The question as it was asked (leg 102):** after a wipe the profile is gone, and with it the answer to the consent screen. §3.3 B wants a restore with "no user action needed"; onboarding's consent text and architecture.md's first-run list say the device ID is read "only after Allow". Both can't hold at a start with no profile.
    - **(a) Look for a backup first (recommended):** at a start with no profile the TV reads its ID, derives the keys and asks the API once. A backup found means this TV's owner allowed it before the wipe, so it restores at once ("Welcome back"). None found: nothing derived is kept, and onboarding asks as it does now. The ID itself still never leaves the TV; the server sees one lookup of a random-looking ID. The consent screen and the privacy text would say "to find and encrypt your backup".
    - **(b) Ask first:** language, disclaimer and "Allow" again after every wipe, then the restore. The promise stays literal; the restore takes three screens instead of none.
- [x] 32a. **The backup API** (`apps/web`, `@lablo/protocol`'s header name).
  - **The shape (leg 102, written before building):**
    - **`src/server/backup.ts`:**
      - `putBackup`: `PUT /v1/backup/:lookupId` with `X-Write-Token`. The lookup ID through `lookupIdSchema` (404), the token through `tokenHash` (401), then the body through `backupEnvelopeSchema` with `BACKUP_BODY_MAX_BYTES` (413, 400). One statement inserts the row or, when the stored hash is the token's, updates its size and date; no row back is 401. Then the envelope's own four fields, as JSON, go to the blob `backup/<lookupId>`. Then the sweep. Answers `{state: 'stored'}`.
      - `getBackup`: `GET`, no token: the blob's bytes as `application/json`, or 404.
      - `deleteBackup`: `DELETE` with the token: 404 with no row, 401 for another token; the blob goes first, then the row. Answers `{state: 'deleted'}`.
      - `sweepBackups(deps, now, max)`: rows last updated `BACKUP_TTL_MS` (180 days) or more ago, oldest first, at most `BACKUP_SWEEP_MAX` a call: each blob, then the rows.
      - Rate limits per caller through `rateKey`: `backup-read` and `backup-write`, 30 in 10 minutes each (a TV reads once at a start with no profile and writes at most every 5 minutes; several TVs may share one address).
      - Every answer with the TV's CORS and `no-store`; `backupRoute` answers 503 as `pairRoute` does, logging only the error's kind.
    - **`src/app/v1/backup/[lookupId]/route.ts`:** `PUT`, `GET`, `DELETE`, and `OPTIONS` allowing `content-type, x-write-token` (`preflight` takes the allowed headers).
    - **`@lablo/protocol`:** `BACKUP_WRITE_HEADER` (`x-write-token`), for the TV in 32c and the server.
  - **Done-when checks (runnable):**
    - `pnpm test:web` (`apps/web/test/backup.web.test.ts`, a real Postgres): a put then a get gives exactly the envelope, sealed and opened with `@lablo/crypto`'s keys from a master; the row holds the token's hash and the size, never the token; a second put with the token replaces, keeping `createdAt`; another token, none, or a malformed one: 401 and the stored envelope unchanged; a malformed lookup ID 404; a body that is no envelope 400, one over 512 KB 413, the longest one stored; two first puts at once with different tokens: one 200, one 401; get of nothing 404, and with the database gone still 200; delete (404, 401, then 200 and both the row and the blob gone, and the ID free to claim again); the sweep at 180 days, bounded, leaving a backup updated since; each rate limit with `Retry-After`, keyed on an HMAC; the route's four methods, its preflight and its 503; no marker of the plaintext in any row or blob;
    - `pnpm test` (the header's name; `preflight`'s headers); lint, typecheck; `pnpm build:web`; `pnpm e2e:web` unchanged;
    - plus the common items (mutation with the list written first, the PR merged on green CI with its `web` job, the docs: architecture.md §3.2 "Built in step 32a", runbook `web.md`).
  - **Built in leg 102** (architecture.md §3.2 "Built in step 32a", runbook `web.md` "The backup"): as the shape says, with these choices: `BackupDeps.db` is a function, asked for only by the calls that write, so a get never opens the database; the sweep runs before the put's own write (a TV back after 180 days has its old row swept and claims its ID again in the same call); the sweep deletes the rows only while they are still old (a put during the sweep keeps its row); a token is refused before the body is read; and one thing the shape didn't plan: `apps/web/e2e/backup.spec.ts`, one test over HTTP against `next start` in `e2e:web`'s `phone` project (a TV calling with `Origin: null`: the preflight, put, get and open, another token refused, delete), since the route tests call the handlers directly.

  - The device secret: Tizen's DUID, webOS's LGUDID through luna (its ACG group in `LUNA_ACG`), read only after "Allow" at the consent screen; else the backup code (made, kept, read back). `deriveBackupMaster` in a worker (which worker, by measured size: main.js has about 41 KB left for steps 32 and 33, and scrypt takes 1 s on Chromium 53 and 3 s on 56), the master cached in IndexedDB, `backupKeys` from it. Unit tests, the worker bundle in the Chromium 53 realm, `build:tv` sizes, `verify:engines`; plus the common items.
  - **The shape (leg 103, written before building):**
    - **The secret is read on the main thread** (Tizen's `webapis` and webOS's luna bridge exist only there): `backupRequest(platform, consent)` in `apps/tv/src/backup/backup-request.ts` calls `platform.deviceSecret()` only when the profile's `consent` is `true`; with `false` or `null` it never calls it. A secret that is `null`, empty or a rejection counts as none. It gives `{platform, appId, secret}`.
    - **`__APP_ID__`**, a new build constant (`appIdFor(platform)` in `tv-defines.config.ts`, read from the manifests): webOS `appinfo.json`'s `id` (`com.lablo.iptv`), Tizen `config.xml`'s application id (`LabloIPTV1.Lablo`), the desktop build webOS's. A test pins it to the manifests: the salt's `appId` can never change once a TV has a backup.
    - **The backup worker** (`packages/data/src/backup.worker.ts`, `backup-service.ts`): a worker of its own, started for each call and ended after it, as the config worker is (its sizes measured against the config worker's with scrypt added, recorded below). `prepare({platform, appId, secret})` answers `{source: 'device' | 'code', derived}` and pushes `progress` (the fraction, in whole percents):
      - **source `device`** when `secret` isn't `null`; else **`code`**: the kept backup code (`kv` `backup.code`), or a new one (16 bytes of `randomBytes`), written before anything is derived from it. A code that can't be kept fails the call: a master from a code nobody can see again would back up what can never be restored.
      - **the cached master** (`kv` `backup.master`: `{source, check, master}`, `check` = b64url(SHA-256(UTF-8(salt + "\|" + secret)))): used when `check` matches the request's; else scrypt runs and the record is replaced (a consent given later, or a code typed in 32d, derives again). A master that can't be written still answers this call. A broken record is replaced.
      - **No key leaves the worker:** 32c's upload and restore run there too.
    - **`@lablo/crypto`'s `backup-code.ts`:** the code is the 128-bit number in base 32, big-endian, as 26 Crockford characters (the first one 0 to 7); `readBackupCode(typed)` drops spaces and dashes, upper-cases, reads I and L as 1 and O as 0, and answers the 26 characters or `null` (32d's typing screen uses it). The secret scrypt takes is those 26 characters.
    - **The engine:** `DataEngine.prepareBackup(request, onProgress)` in a backup worker of its own (`startBackupWorker`), not in the refresh slot.
    - **webOS:** `deviceinfo.query` in the app's `requiredACG` (the app's source now calls `deviceSecret`).
    - **The vectors page** (`dist/tv-vectors`) gets a third run, the bundled backup worker: the vectors' `backup[0]` secret gives their master in IndexedDB, a second run reads it back without scrypt, and a run with no secret makes a code and reads it back; `verify:engines` reads it on Chromium 53 and 68 here, 56 on CI.
    - **Not in 32b:** when the app calls `prepareBackup` (32c: the upload's first run, and the restore at a start with no profile).
  - **Done-when checks (runnable):**

- [x] 32c. **Upload and restore** (`packages/data`, `apps/tv`). Ticked in leg 105 with 32c1 (PR #130) and 32c2 (PR #131).
  - The envelope's content (the profile gzipped, with or without logins) and its size check; the upload 60 s after a change, at most every 5 minutes, and when the app is hidden; at a start with no profile: derive, `GET`, open, check the profile's version (a newer one is refused), save, "Welcome back", re-ingest in the background; a restored file source asking to be sent again. The stand-in API for e2e and the old engines learns the backup routes. architecture.md's storage table corrected. Plus the common items.
  - **Split in leg 104** (more than one leg) into 32c1 (the envelope and the backup worker's `upload` and `restore`, proved in Node, the Chromium 53 realm and the old engines) and 32c2 (the app: the look-up at a start with no profile, "Looking for your backup" and "Welcome back", the upload schedule, the consent text, the stand-in APIs, e2e). 32c is ticked when both are.
  - **Decided in leg 104 (each with its why):**
    - **"Include login details" off moves to 32d, with its toggle.** 32c's envelope always holds the logins (the setting's default is on). Why: a source without its login or link is a new profile shape (a version bump: an older app would read it as a broken source), and it only makes sense with the screen that asks for the login again after a restore; both belong with the toggle in Settings.
    - **The restore writes nothing until a backup is restored.** The look-up derives the master in memory; only a restored profile is written, in one transaction with the master record and the record of what was sent. So "nothing derived is kept" holds by construction, whatever ends the look-up (none found, unreadable, a newer profile, the network, or 32c2's time limit terminating the worker).
    - **A backup this TV hasn't seen is never overwritten.** Before the first `PUT` under a lookup ID (no record of a send under it), the upload asks `GET`: a backup there (a look-up that failed or timed out at the start, or one from a newer app version) holds the upload (`held`), and 32d's Settings offers to restore it or replace it. Why: the lookup ID comes from the device ID, so after a wipe whose look-up failed, the new profile's first upload would otherwise replace the old backup with an almost empty one.
    - **A restore's catalogs come back through the refresh schedule**, which already refreshes every stored source with no catalog (step 22a: webOS's wipe of the database on an update), through `DataEngine.refresh` and the blocklist. The config's `block-new-sources` doesn't refuse them: a restore adds no source the user didn't have, as the update wipe doesn't. (Leg 103's note "a restore's re-ingest is a new way to add sources" is corrected by this.)
- [x] 32c1. **The envelope and the backup worker's upload and restore** (`packages/data`, `apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts`).
  - **The shape (leg 104, written before building):**
    - **The plaintext** (`packages/data/src/backup-plain.ts`): `backupPlain(doc)` is the UTF-8 of the stored profile document's JSON (the current version, whole: sources with their logins, guides, favourites, places, settings), gzipped with fflate's `gzipSync`. `readBackupPlain(bytes)` gunzips (a gzip whose trailer says more than `BACKUP_PLAIN_MAX_BYTES`, 8 MB, is refused before inflating), decodes strict UTF-8 and parses JSON: an object with a whole `v` of 1 or more, else `null`. A phone-sent file source goes as its record is (by name; it has no bytes).
    - **The size check comes before sealing:** gzip plus the 16-byte tag over `BACKUP_CT_MAX_BYTES` is `too-large` and nothing is sent.
    - **`BackupService.upload({platform, appId, secret, base})`** (the request `prepare` takes, plus the API's origin): reads the profile (not written) and answers:
      - `none`: no profile, one from a newer app version, or one with no source yet (an unfinished onboarding is nothing to restore);
      - the master through `prepare` (kept, or derived and kept);
      - `same`: the document's hash (b64url(SHA-256(its JSON))) and the lookup ID are those of the last send (`kv` `backup.sent`): no request;
      - `held`: no send under this lookup ID yet, and `GET` finds a backup there (see "Decided in leg 104");
      - `sent`: `PUT /v1/backup/<lookupId>` with `X-Write-Token` answered 200; `kv` `backup.sent` = `{lookupId, token, hash, old}`;
      - **the old copy:** when the last send was under another lookup ID (a consent given later), it is deleted after the new `PUT` with its own token; 200, 404 and 401 forget it, anything else keeps it in `old` for the next upload;
      - `too-large` (before sealing, or the API's 413), `failed` with the reason (`network`, `timeout`, `status`, `rate`).
    - **`BackupService.restore({platform, appId, secret, base})`** (`secret` is the device ID; `null` is refused: 32d's typed code has its own path):
      - `kept`: a profile exists (looked at first, and again in the write's transaction: never written over);
      - the master derived in memory (`progress` events), nothing written;
      - `none`: `GET` answers 404;
      - `unreadable`: the answer isn't an envelope, doesn't open with `encKey`, or isn't a profile;
      - `newer`: the profile's version is above this app's (the server's copy stays; the upload holds it);
      - `restored` with the profile's `language` and its number of sources: an older version migrated (`migrateProfile`), then written as `current` with the master record and `backup.sent` (so the next upload needs no scrypt and no `GET`, and sends nothing until the profile changes);
      - `failed` (`network`, `timeout`, `status`, `rate`).
    - **Requests:** `fetchTransport` (`PUT` and `DELETE` added to `ByteRequest`), each with `BACKUP_TIMEOUT_MS` (20 s) for its whole answer (Chromium 53 can't abort a fetch) and the answer capped at `BACKUP_BODY_MAX_BYTES` plus 1 KB. The `PUT`'s body is written by hand (the envelope's fields are base64url and constants). Results never hold a key, a token or a URL.
    - **The engine:** `DataEngine.uploadBackup(request)` and `restoreBackup(request, {onProgress, cancel})`, each in a backup worker of its own (as `prepareBackup`), beside the refresh slot; a cancel (32c2's time limit) terminates the worker.
    - **The worker's size** is measured with fflate's gzip and gunzip and the profile's migrations (its budget is 150 KB; 105.5 KB in 32b).
    - **The old engines:** `@lablo/data/check-backup` gains `backup.upload`, `backup.same`, `backup.restore` and `backup.none` against a stand-in transport in the page (the API's answers in memory: no server on the vectors page): the vectors' secret seals, sends, reads back and restores into its own database on Chromium 53, 56 and 68.
  - **Done-when checks (runnable):**
    - `pnpm test`: `backup-plain.test.ts` (round trip; the size edge; the trailer's cap; broken gzip, UTF-8, JSON, no version); `backup-service.test.ts` against fake-indexeddb and a stand-in API with the 32a server's rules (each `upload` and `restore` state above; the token's hash on the stand-in; the first send's `GET`; the old copy deleted, kept, then deleted at the next upload; no profile written before a restore succeeds; a profile written meanwhile never overwritten; the restored profile, master record and `backup.sent` in one transaction; an older backup migrated; the time limit); the engine's two calls; `check-backup.test.ts` with the new names;
    - `test/backup-worker-bundle.test.ts`: the bundled worker within its budget in the Chromium 53 stand-in realm sends and restores into a fresh database;
    - `pnpm build:tv` (the sizes recorded), `e2e/crypto-vectors.spec.ts`, `verify:engines --engine chrome53 --engine chrome68`;
    - plus the common items (lint, typecheck, mutation with the list written first, the PR merged on green CI, the docs).

    - **`backupPlain` takes the JSON text** (the service hashes and gzips the same text once); `readBackupPlain` also refuses a stream whose inflated length isn't its trailer's.
    - **The lookup ID is claimed before the first `PUT`** (`backup.sent` with hash `''`), so a `PUT` that fails is sent again without the `GET`, and a claim that can't be written stops the upload before any `PUT` (else the next upload would find its own backup and hold it for good).
    - **The restore's send record holds the hash of the document as the server has it**, so an older version's backup, restored and upgraded, is sent again at the next upload.
    - **`@lablo/protocol/backup-limits`**, a module that imports nothing: importing the limits from the protocol's index would bring zod into the backup worker.
    - **`backupStandIn`** (`packages/data/src/backup-stand-in.ts`), the API in memory with the 32a server's rules, shared by the unit tests and the vectors page (no server there).
    - **The engine test records the backup worker's URLs**, so the request's base is checked (a mutant that changed it survived the stand-in, which answers any origin).

- [x] 32d2. **Settings' backup page** (`apps/tv`, `packages/data`, `e2e`). Backup on or off (off deletes the server's copy at once; the schedule sends nothing while off); when the master comes from the backup code, the code in groups to photograph; the last send; a held backup's "Restore it" (written over the profile, with a snapshot) or "Replace it". Its shape is written before building.
  - **Split in leg 107** (two legs' work, as 32c was) into 32d2a (the backup worker's and the data engine's side) and 32d2b (the page, the schedule's part, e2e). 32d2 is ticked when both are.
  - **Decided in leg 107 (each with its why; the build may refine one and writes that down):**
    - **On or off lives in the backup worker's `kv` (`backup.off`), not in the profile.** Why: the profile is what a backup sends and restores, and any backup that exists was made while on, so the field would only ever come back as "on"; it would also cost a profile version (32d4 takes 9, 32e 10). A wipe forgets it as it forgets everything, and the onboarding after a wipe shows the backup disclosure again ("on by default, off in Settings"). **32d4's "include login details" is the other way round:** it goes in the profile (its version 9), because a backup made without logins must bring the choice back with it, or the logins typed again after a restore would be sent.
    - **Off deletes at once, and keeps trying.** `backup.off` is written first (no upload starts after it), then the copy under the lookup ID this TV writes and every old copy are deleted with their own tokens (`DELETE`, 32a). A delete that fails stays in `backup.sent` and is tried again by each later schedule run, which then answers `off` without a `GET` or a `PUT`. On again: the next run sends as a first send does (`GET`, then the claim and `PUT`; a copy whose delete never went through is still this TV's, so it is `same` or sent over, never `held`).
    - **The page asks the worker for the backup's status** when it opens: on or off; the master's source (`device` after "Allow", else `code`); the backup code only when the source is the code (made and kept then if there is none yet, as `prepare` does); when the last send went (`backup.sent` gains `at`; a record without it reads as "not yet"); whether deletes are still pending. The code goes to the page as 26 characters and is drawn in groups (`dir="ltr"`), with one line on what it is for. Why the worker: it alone reads `kv`'s backup records, and the code is made in one transaction there (`keepCode`).
    - **A held backup** (`held`: a backup this TV hasn't sent is under its lookup ID) gets two answers in the worker: **"Restore it"** reads and opens it as the look-up does, then writes it over the current profile in one transaction with the current document kept as a snapshot (reason `restore`), and `backup.sent` names that copy as sent (so the next run sends only a change); a backup from a newer app version is refused (`newer`) and nothing changes. **"Replace it"** claims the lookup ID and `PUT`s the current profile (the TV derives the same write token, so the API accepts it). Why both in the worker: they need the master and the keys, which never leave it.
    - **After "Restore it" the app starts again** as after a restore at a first start ("Welcome back" in the restored language, then the live screen with the restored sources' catalogs read at once, `start(0)`). Why: every screen holds the old profile's sources and catalogs; a start is the one path already proved to rebuild them all.

- [x] 32d3a. **The TV bundle as UTF-8** (`tools/build`).
  - **Decided in leg 109 (with its why):** terser writes the bundles as UTF-8 (`ascii_only: false`), not the catalogs in a file of their own. Why: it frees the room at once for every screen to come (35.5 KB of main.js: 9,076 escapes, nearly all Arabic letters at 6 bytes, now 2; the handover's 45 KB guess counted the escapes but not the 2 bytes each still costs), with one changed line and no new loading path. The risk is an engine reading main.js in another charset; it can't break the code (non-ASCII is only ever inside string and regex literals, so a wrong charset garbles text, never syntax), and every TV page declares `<meta charset="utf-8">` before its scripts, which a `file://` script without a charset of its own follows (Blob workers are always UTF-8). Measured before deciding: terser 5.51 with `ascii_only: false` still escapes U+2028 and U+2029 (raw, they end an ES2016 literal; the syntax gate refuses them), U+FEFF and lone surrogates (no UTF-8 form; written raw the file would hold U+FFFD), in strings and in regexes; an astral pair is written as one 4-byte character.

Else `kept`, and nothing is derived.
    - **The master is derived in memory** from the typed code (scrypt, `progress`), the backup fetched and opened as `restore` does (`none`, `unreadable`, `newer`, `failed` answered as they are, nothing written). Why in memory: a mistyped code that finds nothing must leave nothing derived behind, as the device ID's look-up does.

    - **The answer** is `BackupRestoreResult` as it is (`restored` with the language and the sources' count), so the screen (32d3c) reuses 32d2b's restart (`restartUrl` with `restored=1`: "Welcome back", then the live screen).

The harness's builds have no device ID, so their backup goes under a code: the walk adds a source by phone with "Not now", uploads, reads the code from the app's IndexedDB (an ES5 read through CDP, no test hook that would show the code), wipes, walks onboarding to the new row, types the code by key events (char events on 53), and checks "Welcome back" and the live screen as 32d1's walk does, in English and Arabic on Chromium 53, 56 and 68.   - **The shape (leg 110, written before building; each with its why; the build may refine one and writes that down):**
    - **A walk of its own** (`tools/engines/src/code-walk.ts`, `runCodeWalk`), beside 32d1's `backup-walk.ts`, reusing its pieces (the pair server's `BackupStandIn` on the container's 127.0.0.1:`PAIR_PORT`, the phone's payload, `resetDatabase`, `STALE_MARK`, the live screen's row and rail checks, the safe-area and cut-label layout probe). Why its own file: 32d1's walk is built around the simulated LGUDID (the look-up, "Looking for your backup"); this one has no device ID at all, and mixing the two would branch every step.
    - **On the plain Tizen build** (`dist/tv/tizen` staged as the `app` variant), not the simulated webOS copy: the plain builds read no device ID on an engine (no luna bridge, no `webapis`), which is exactly a TV after "Not now", and it gives the other platform a restore walk (32d1 walked webOS). The salt's platform is `tizen`.
    - **Each walk** (English, then Arabic), from a fresh database: onboarding by phone as the pairing walk, but with **"Not now"** (RIGHT in English, LEFT in Arabic) → "Use your phone" (ArrowDown twice) → the scripted phone sends the fixture's playlist → done → "Start watching"; `uploadBackup()` → `sent` (`GET 404`, `PUT 200`); **the code read from the app's database** by an ES5 expression through CDP (`kv` `backup.code`, as `PROFILE_EXPRESSION` reads the profile), never through a test hook (the app must not grow a way to show the code outside Settings); **the wipe** (`resetDatabase` from the blank page, the stale mark, the same address again): onboarding at once (no look-up: no device ID; the stand-in asked nothing); the language, the disclaimer, "Not now", ArrowDown three times to "Use a backup code", OK → `restore-code/field/0`.
    - **Typing:** OK opens the field's edit; the text goes as `char` key events (`typeText`, as the form walk does: `Input.insertText` is missing on 53), Enter ends the edit (the focus moves to "Restore"). **First a mistyped code** (the real one with its last character changed, still a valid code): `none`, "No backup was found", the focus back on the field, nothing written; it proves the walk can fail without a seed of its own (a wrong code is the failure a person makes). Then the field cleared (Backspace ×32 as `keyDown`, which the form walk already uses) and **the real code in lower case in groups of four with spaces**.
    - **The checks:** "Opening your backup…" seen or skipped (scrypt is 0.9 s on 53), then "Welcome back" in the walk's language with one source (inside the title-safe area, no label cut; a screenshot), then the live screen by itself with its rows filled, its rail, count and rows as the fixture says (a screenshot); the code screen with the typed code drawn (`data-phase`, the input's value in groups, its `dir`; a screenshot, inside the safe area, nothing cut, in Arabic too); the profile read back holds the phone's source, the walk's language and `consent: false`; `kv`'s code equals the one read before the wipe; the stand-in answered `GET 404, PUT 200, GET 404, GET 200`, the last three under the lookup ID the code gives (`backupKeys(deriveBackupMaster(code, 'tizen', appId))`), the mistyped one under another.
    - **Where it runs:** a screen of the `app` variant on the Tizen bundle after the backup walk, on Chromium 53, 56 and 68 (`verify-engines.ts`), with its summary line (the code's restore time, "Welcome back" and live times) and the runbook's item.

    - **What "login details" are:** an Xtream source's user name and password; an M3U source's link, whole; each of the user's guide links, whole. Kept: every id, name, order, favourite, place, offset and pin, an Xtream source's server (`base`: no login in it, `normalizeXtreamBase`), and a link's host and port (what the sources and guides screens already show, never the path or the query). A phone-sent file has no login (its name only). Why the whole link: a playlist or guide link carries its login in its path or its query as often as not, and nothing tells the two apart; the toggle's promise is that no login leaves the TV, so a link has to count as one. Its line in Settings says so ("logins and links").
    - **Where the choice lives: the profile** (`backupLogins: false`; no field is on), as leg 107 decided: a backup made without logins brings the choice back with it, so logins typed again after a restore aren't sent. It travels in the stripped envelope too.
    - **Profile version 9:** a source may lack its login (Xtream: its server and no account; M3U: its host and no link) and a guide may lack its link (its host). The migration from 8 changes no data (every version 8 source has its login); it is the version an older app refuses (`newer`), since it can't use a source without a login. 32e takes version 10. The pins: `profile.test.ts`, `schema.test.ts` if it pins it, `query-service.test.ts`, `test/onboarding-loader-mock.test.ts`, `e2e/episode-resume.spec.ts` (grep `v: 8`).
    - **The stripped envelope:** the worker strips before gzip and sealing (`withoutLogins`), from the stored document as it is (nothing written back). `same` compares the stripped plaintext's hash, so turning the toggle sends at the next run; the page asks for that run at once (`uploadNow`, as turning the backup on does), and the `PUT` replaces the copy with logins under the same lookup ID (the API keeps one blob a lookup ID). On again sends the logins again.
    - **A source or guide without its login is never refreshed:** the data engine refuses its refresh (`needs-login`, beside the blocklist's refusal), so no URL is built from a missing login; its catalog and guide stay empty until the login comes back. Why in the engine: every refresh goes through `DataEngine.refresh` (the gate the blocklist uses), so no screen can start one by mistake.
    - **The way back (32d4b):** after a restore with sources missing their logins, "Welcome back" says so in one line and its button opens Settings › Sources instead of the live screen. The sources screen marks each such row "Needs its login" (a guide on the guides screen "Needs its link"); OK opens a form: Xtream the user name and password under the server it kept (the server editable), M3U the link (its host as the hint), a guide the link; the onboarding form's checks (`checkForm`, `normalizeLink`); "Save" writes it (`editProfile`) and refreshes that source (the scheduler's `refreshNow`), and the row shows the refresh's answer (a refused login says so; OK opens the form again). The toggle on Settings › Backup: a second row "Include logins and links" while the backup is on, with its line; off sends at once and asks nothing (it removes, it doesn't lose).
    - **The harness:** `check-backup`'s worker checks gain an upload without logins (the opened plaintext has no login and no link, the hosts kept) and its restore (sources and guides without logins, `backupLogins: false` back); the old engines run them in the vectors page. 32d4b's e2e: toggle off, upload, the envelope opened in Node holds no login (`e2e:web`'s `tv` project, as `backup-api.spec.ts` opens it), the wipe, "Welcome back" with its line, Sources, the login given again, the channels back, and the next upload still without it.
  - **Refined while building 32d4a (leg 112):**
    - **The channels played last hold the login too:** each keeps its channel's catalog item, whose stream URL (`/live/<user>/<password>/…`) and catch-up template carry it. The stripped envelope keeps them with `url: ''` and `catchup: null`: Home's "Recent channels" plays from the catalog by key, so it works again once the login is back, and the start doesn't play a last channel with no URL (`lastChannelOf`). Why not drop them: the recent channels are the user's, and only the URL is a login.

A guide or an Xtream panel without one isn't listed by `epgGuides` (no URL can be built), so `refreshEpg` never reads it. - [x] 32e. **Logins at rest under `localKey`** (`packages/data`'s profile, `apps/tv`). Decided by Omar in leg 115: no seal at rest (ADR-013); docs only.
  - As decided above: profile version 10 (32d4 takes 9; leg 106), each source's login or link sealed under `localKey` and opened only when a URL is built; its stated limit. Plus the common items.
  - **Found while writing the shape (leg 115): sealing the profile would not keep the login off the TV's disk.** The login is stored in plain text in more places than the profile, and two of them are large:
    - **every catalog item's stream URL** (`catalog_chunks`, up to 200k items a source): an Xtream item's `url` is `xtreamStreamUrl(account, …)` (`/live/<user>/<password>/<id>.ts`), and an M3U item keeps the provider's URL as given (often `/<user>/<password>/<id>` or a token in the query; nothing tells the two apart, as 32d4's shape says of links);
    - `sources_meta.epgUrls` (an M3U's `url-tvg`, often the panel's `xmltv.php?username=…&password=…`);
    - the profile's sources and guides, `player.last.item` and every `channels[].item` (32d4a).
  - **And the key would sit beside it:** `localKey` comes from the master, which `kv` caches in the same IndexedDB (§3.3 B "Found while building, for 32e"). Not caching it costs scrypt at every start before the first play (1 s on Chromium 53, 3.4 s on 56, perhaps 10+ s on a 2018 TV), against the plan's "opens on the last channel in under 3 seconds". Wrapping the cached master under a key from the device ID read at each start avoids that, but whoever can copy a TV app's database (Dev Mode root, a flash dump) holds the TV and its ID too; and on a TV that said "Not now", the only secret (the backup code) is in that same database.
  - **So the options are:**
    - **(A) No seal at rest on the TV, said plainly (recommended):** the logins stay in the app's own storage on the TV, which other apps can't read; what stays encrypted is everything that leaves the TV (the phone pairing end to end, the backup sealed on the TV with "Include logins and links" to leave them out; our servers never hold a login in plain text). 32e becomes docs: ADR-006's "Credentials on the TV are encrypted with `localKey`" and architecture.md §3.3 B amended, PLAN.md's "Credentials are encrypted on the TV" (§ "Trustworthy") and risk item 8 reworded with Omar's word, profile.ts's "step 32 encrypts it" comments fixed; `localKey` stays derived but unused. About a third of a leg; then step 33.
    - **(B) Seal every copy:** the profile's logins and links, the recents' URLs, `epgUrls`, and every stored stream URL (Xtream items stored without the account and built at play time; M3U items' URLs sealed in their chunks), under a `localKey` from a master wrapped by the device ID. Protects a copied database only on a TV that allowed the device ID and only from someone without the TV. 4 to 6 legs (the catalog's ingest version and every reader: query worker, player, catch-up, EPG, recents, backup, the old-engine proofs), a device-ID read and a decrypt before the first play at every start, and main.js has 25 KB left on Tizen.
    - **(C) The original 32e only** (the profile and the recents sealed under `localKey` from the cached master): about 2 legs, and the password stays in plain text in every stored stream URL beside it. This is the "pretend" step 32's decision rules out.
  - **Omar chose (A) on 2026-11-19 (leg 115, in its tab: "1").** 32e is docs only:
    - `docs/adr/ADR-013-logins-on-the-tv-unsealed.md` (the decision, why, the options not taken), listed in the ADR index;
    - ADR-006 amended (its status and its "Credentials on the TV" line);
    - architecture.md §3.3 B: the "Credentials on the TV" line, the "Found while building, for 32e" note and a new "Decided in step 32e" block, plus the three step notes that said "step 32 encrypts it";
    - PLAN.md, with Omar's answer as his word: §4 "Trustworthy", risk item 8 and the storage plan;
    - the comments in `profile.ts`, `engine-loader.ts` and crypto's `backup.ts` (`localKey`: derived and unused).

- [x] 33. **Telemetry.** Telemetry, URL/credential scrubber, crash handler, diagnostics support code. Ticked in leg 120: 33a, 33b, 33c and 33d all merged (PRs #143 to #149).
  - **Split in leg 115** (four legs' work, as 32 was) into:
    - 33a: the scrubber and the telemetry API;
    - 33b: the TV's telemetry: its id, the queue, the sending, sampling, the events and the Settings switch;
    - 33c: the crash handler;
    - 33d: the diagnostics report and its support code.

    33 is ticked when all four are.
  - **Decided in leg 115** (each with its why; a later leg may refine one where building shows more, and writes that down):
    - **The scrubber lives in `@lablo/protocol`, schema-free, at a subpath of its own (`@lablo/protocol/scrub`).** The TV (main and workers) and the server both use it. Why: both sides need the same rules and tests, and a schema-free subpath keeps zod out of the workers that import it (leg 104's lesson with `backup-limits`).
    - **The rules, in order:**
      1. On the TV, the user's own logins, links and hosts, matched exactly and longest first. The TV passes them from the profile's sources and guides; strings under 4 characters are skipped.
      2. Every URL (`scheme://` up to the next space, quote or bracket) becomes `[url]`. The app's own script frames (`file://…/<name>.js:<line>:<col>`, and `blob:` worker frames) are kept, cut to the file's name and position: they are what makes a stack worth having.
      3. `user=`, `username=`, `password=`, `pass=`, `token=` and the like become `<key>=[x]`.
      4. IPv4 and IPv6 addresses, with a port, become `[ip]`.
      5. `x@y` becomes `[email]`.
      6. Dotted names whose last label is a top-level domain from a list (the common generic ones and every two-letter country code) become `[host]`.

      Then the text is cut to the schema's 4,000 characters. Why over-redact: a crash with `[host]` where `e.id` was is still useful, and a host that leaks breaks §3.4's promise. A channel or programme name has no shape to find: 33c checks that the app's own error messages carry none.
    - **The server keeps only what the schema keeps.** `readTelemetryEvents` strips unknown fields, and `crash.stack` is scrubbed again (the generic rules: the server knows no login). Each event becomes one row in `TelemetryEvent`; no migration, since step 29a's table has every column. The caller's address is used only through `rateKey`. Retention is 90 days (§3.2), swept a bounded number of rows at each post, as backups are.
    - **The install ID** is 16 random bytes in `kv` (`telemetry.id`), made at first need. It is never in the backup, so a wipe gives a new one (§3.4). Why `kv`: the profile travels in the backup.
    - **Sending runs in a worker of its own, started for each send** (as the backup worker):
      - main records events in memory (at most 200) and hands them over at the next send;
      - the worker appends them to the queue in `kv` (`telemetry.queue`, at most 200, oldest dropped first), posts all of it, and clears what was sent on a 200.

      Why: main.js has 25 KB left on Tizen, and the sender needs `kv` and the transport, which the workers already have.
    - **When it sends:** 60 s after the first event not yet sent, at most every 15 minutes, and when the app is hidden (as the backup's schedule does). Sampling is per kind, from the config's `telemetrySampling` (none means all). It is decided per install (a hash of the install ID and the kind), so an install always sends a kind or never does.
    - **The switch: Settings, "Share anonymous usage and crash reports", on by default** (§3.4's opt-out). It is kept in the profile as `telemetry: false`, an optional field with no version bump (as `noticesShown`), so a restore brings an "off" back. Off records nothing and clears the queue. The consent screen gets one more sentence about it (en, ar). Counsel reviews opt-out against Quebec's Law 25 and the GCC PDPLs before launch (PLAN.md's legal weeks); a later opt-in would change the default, not the design.
    - **The diagnostics report (33d)** is a scrubbed summary, sent only when the user asks ("Send a report" in Settings). The answer is a 6-character code to give support. Step 40's diagnostics screen reuses it.

      - it is ES2017 with no regex feature Chromium 53 lacks (no lookbehind, no named groups, no `s` flag);
      - the list of top-level domains is a constant.
    - **`apps/web/src/server/telemetry.ts`, `postTelemetry`:**

      - `readTelemetryEvents`, each `crash.stack` through `scrubText`;
      - the sweep (`TELEMETRY_TTL_MS`, 90 days; `TELEMETRY_SWEEP_MAX` rows a post), then one `createMany`;
      - answers 200 `{state: 'stored', stored, dropped}`, with the TV's CORS and `no-store`, and 503 as the other routes do.
    - **The route** `src/app/v1/telemetry/route.ts`: `POST` and `OPTIONS`.

    2. **An Xtream path without its URL** loses its two login segments (a rule of its own).

Its shape is written before building. Ticked in leg 118: 33b1, 33b2 and 33b3 all merged (PRs #144, #145, #146).
  - **Split in leg 116** (three legs' work) into:
    - 33b1: the sender (the worker, the install ID, the queue, sampling, the schedule, the profile's switch) and the two start events, `app_start` and `capability`;
    - 33b2: the four other events (`ingest_done`, `epg_match`, `play_result`, `stall`);
    - 33b3: the switch's Settings row and the consent screen's sentence.

    33b is ticked when all three are.
  - **Decided in leg 116** (each with its why; leg 115's decisions above still hold):
    - **Buckets without zod:** `COUNT_BUCKETS`, `countBucket`, `RATE_BUCKETS`, `rateBucket`, `TELEMETRY_KINDS`, `TELEMETRY_BATCH_MAX` and `TELEMETRY_BODY_MAX_BYTES` move to a schema-free subpath, `@lablo/protocol/telemetry-buckets` (the `exports` entry, the lint entry point and its test), and `telemetry.ts` re-exports them. Why: main and the worker both need the buckets, and the `@lablo/protocol` index pulls zod. Main takes the event types with `import type` only.
    - **A worker of its own** (`packages/data/src/telemetry.worker.ts`, `telemetryHandlers` in `telemetry-service.ts`, `createTelemetryClient`), started for each send and ended when it answers, as the backup worker is. Why: the backup worker is 105.5 KB and holds keys; the config worker runs on its own schedule. A small worker of its own leaves room for 33c's scrubber (25.8 KB) and can't hold anything else up. Its size is measured in a bundle test against the 150 KB budget.
    - **The worker's one call, `send({events, base, platform, appVersion, sampling})`:**
      1. **The switch first:** it reads the stored profile's raw `telemetry` field (`storedProfileDoc`). `false`: it deletes `telemetry.queue` and `telemetry.id`, posts nothing and answers `off`. Why: the worker enforces the switch itself (a restore brings an "off" back; main's copy could be stale), and deleting the ID means rows from before an "off" and after an "on" can't be linked.
      2. **The install ID** (`telemetry.id` in `kv`): 16 bytes from `crypto.getRandomValues`, unpadded base64url (22 characters, `bytes16`), made at first need.
      3. **Sampling:** an event is kept when `fnv1a(installId + ':' + kind) / 2^32 < sampling[kind]` (a kind with no rate is kept). Why: per install, so an install always sends a kind or never does (§3.4), and FNV-1a is already in the data package; no secrecy is needed.
      4. **`capability` once per change:** kept only when its fields differ from the last one queued (`telemetry.capability` in `kv`). Why: it changes only with an app or firmware update, and an app starts several times a day.
      5. **The queue** (`telemetry.queue` in `kv`): the kept events appended, the newest 200 kept, in one transaction.
      6. **The post:** the oldest events whose JSON fits under `TELEMETRY_BODY_MAX_BYTES` less 1 KB for the envelope (UTF-8 bytes), at most 200, `POST <base>/v1/telemetry` with a 20 s limit, as the backup's requests. The body is built with `JSON.stringify` here: the worker isn't main.js, and a crash's stack (33c) needs escaping. Its polyfill cost is in the measured size.
      7. **The answer:** 200 removes the posted events from the queue's front. 400 and 413 remove them too, because the server will never take them (logged). 429, 5xx, a network failure or the time limit keep them. The worker answers `{state: 'sent' | 'empty' | 'off' | 'failed', sent, queued, failure?}`, and never puts a URL in it.

      A send killed mid-post may send its events twice: the server has no dedupe, and telemetry is best effort.
    - **The browser build sends as `platform: 'browser'`.** The real API answers 400 (its `platform` is `tizen` or `webos`), so the batch is dropped. Plain e2e's stand-in takes it and checks the events with `readTelemetryEvents`, and the envelope with the platform swapped. Why: the dev app must exercise the same path, and dev rows must never reach the real table. So there is no real-API TV spec. 33a's `e2e:web` already covers the route over HTTP.
    - **Main** (`apps/tv/src/telemetry/telemetry-state.ts`, `createTelemetry`):
      - `record(event)` keeps plain event objects in memory, at most 200 (the oldest dropped), unless the switch is off.
      - The first event after `load(profile)` reads the switch (`profile.doc.telemetry !== false`). Events recorded before it are held, and dropped when it says off.
    - **When it sends** (as `backup-schedule.ts`):
      - **After an event:** 60 s after the first one not yet sent, and never sooner than 15 minutes after the last send began.
      - **After a `failed` answer** that still has a queue: once more, 15 minutes later.
      - **Hidden:** one send at once, if anything is pending.
      - **One at a time:** an event recorded during a send waits for the next.
      - **A dead worker** puts the events back in memory.
      - **The request:** each send asks the request afresh. The base is the backup's (`configRequest.base`), and the sampling is `config.policy`'s `tunables.telemetrySampling` (none: all).
      - **`disable-app` stops it,** as it stops the backup. Why: a stopped app does nothing in the background.
      - **The test hook:** `idle()` counts its worker (e2e wipes the database only when it is idle), and `telemetryNow()` sends at once.
    - **The switch in the profile:** `telemetry?: false` (optional, no version bump, as `noticesShown`), with the edit `{kind: 'telemetry', on}` (as `backupLogins`). It travels in the backup, so a restore brings an "off" back. Off in main drops the memory and asks one send, so the worker clears the queue at once. The Settings row and the consent sentence are 33b3's.
    - **`app_start`** is recorded once the start screen has painted:
      - `tier` from the platform's info;
      - `coldStartMs`: `performance.now()` in the first animation frame after the start screen renders, rounded and capped at the schema's hour;
      - `catalog`: `countBucket` of the stored items, the sum of the sources' `counts`.

      A restart for "Welcome back" counts as a start.
    - **`capability`** is recorded at each start (the worker drops the repeats):
      - `model` and `firmware` from the platform's info, with characters outside `deviceName`'s set replaced by `_`, cut to 64, and `unknown` when empty;
      - `chromium`: `chromiumVersion`;
      - `probes`: `year` (the model year, when known), `lowTier`, `mse` (`MediaSource` exists), `hevc` (`canPlayType` of an HEVC MP4 type).

      Why so few probes: each must earn its place in an engine decision. The screen reader's state stays out: it is a disability signal.

- [x] 33b2. **The four other events** (`packages/player`, `apps/tv`, `packages/data`). Merged in leg 117 (PR #145). Its shape is refined before building:
  - `ingest_done` from `DataEngine.refresh` (`xtream` or `m3u`, `elapsedMs`, the download's bytes and the items as buckets) and `refreshEpg` (`epg`);
  - `epg_match` after a guide refresh (the share of live channels with a guide, `rateBucket`);
  - `play_result` from the player controller (engine, container, mode, time to the first frame, or the failure's kind, code and status), once per play;
  - `stall` at a stall's end (recovered, attempts).

  Each has unit tests. The e2e stand-in sees a `play_result` after a play and an `ingest_done` after onboarding.
  - **Facts found in leg 116** (for the shape; nothing decided yet):
    - **The sender is there.** Each event is one `telemetry.record(...)` in main (`apps/tv/src/telemetry/telemetry-state.ts`), built by a hook-free function next to `telemetry-events.ts`. Test it with `serverReads` (`readTelemetryEvents` keeps it whole), as `telemetry-events.test.ts` does.
    - **`ingest_done`, catalogs:** `IngestResult` has `elapsedMs`, `fetch` (`FetchStamp.bytes`; `null` on a 304), `counts` per kind, `status` (`committed` / `unchanged`). The source type comes from the refresh's source (`xtream`, or `m3u` for a link or the phone's file). The results reach main through `DataEngine.refresh` (the refresh scheduler, onboarding's loader, the phone's add, a login given again).
    - **`ingest_done`, guides:** each `EpgOutcome.result` has `elapsedMs`, `fetch.bytes`, and `summary` (`EpgSummary`: `wanted`, `matched`, `programmes`, `kept`); `refreshEpg` gives `EpgRefreshResult.outcomes`.
    - **`epg_match`:** `EpgSummary` is per guide. The share of live channels with any guide needs the union: the unmatched count ("Channels without a guide", `unmatched-state.ts`) over the live channels, or a new query-worker count. Decide which.
    - **`play_result` and `stall`:**
      - the controller's `subscribe(status)` gives `state` (`playing`, `reconnecting`, `failed`, `ended`…), `session`, `container`, `attempt` and `failure` (`category`, `code`, `detail`);
      - `StreamStats.engine` is `'avplay' | 'html5' | 'mse'`, the protocol's three;
      - the engine's `PlaybackErrorKind` looks like the protocol's `error.kind` list (check it is the same seven);
      - the time to the first frame needs the play's start time;
      - the mode (live, VOD, catch-up) is the app's (`app-player.ts`, `player-state.ts`);
      - a `failure.code` must fit the protocol's `errorCode` pattern.
  - **Decided in leg 117** (written before building, each with its why):
    - **`play_result` and `stall` come from the controller** (`packages/player`), through a new option, `ControllerOptions.report(report: PlayerReport)`; `appPlayer` passes the app's on. Why: only the controller knows a play's start, its first frame, the `PlaybackError` behind a give-up and the engine that played. The status's `failure` keeps only the category, the code and the detail, so reading it through `subscribe` would mean adding all of that to the status.
      - `PlayerReport` is `{type: 'play', engine, container, mode, ttffMs, error}` or `{type: 'stall', recovered, attempts}`; `error` is the `PlaybackError` as the engine or the watchdog gave it.
      - **A play is a session** (from `play()` to the next `play` or `stop`). It reports once:
        - at its first frame: `ttffMs` from `play()` to that frame on the controller's clock, `error: null`;
        - or at its give-up before any frame: `ttffMs: null`, the last error.

        A play left before either (a zap, a stop) reports nothing. Why: it says nothing about the stream, and a quick zap through channels would flood the queue. A play the blocklist refuses reports nothing either: that is the config's verdict, not playback, and an event would tie the install to a blocked provider.
      - **A play hidden while it opens** starts its clock again on show, where the controller opens it again. Why: a TV left in the background must not read as an hour's open.
      - **The fields:** `engine` is `engine.kind` at that moment (the fallback engine names the one that played or tried last); `container` is the candidate's, `'other'` when the request had no URL (`NO_URL`); `mode` is the request's.
      - **A stall** is any failure after the session showed a frame: the retries under "Reconnecting…". It ends at the next first frame (`recovered: true`) or at the give-up (`recovered: false`), and `attempts` is the retries it took (the session's `attempt`). Not stalls: a resume after a hide (`reconnecting` with no failure), and a buffering the stream gets over by itself. A stall left by a zap or a stop reports nothing: the schema has only "recovered" and "failed".
      - **A report that throws** changes nothing in playback (caught and logged). Why: telemetry must never break a play.
    - **The player's events in main** (`apps/tv/src/telemetry/telemetry-events.ts`, plain functions as 33b1's):
      - `playEvent(at, report)`:
        - `ttffMs` rounded and capped at the schema's hour;
        - the error's `kind` as it is: the engine's seven are the protocol's seven, checked both ways by a type test;
        - its `code` kept when it is 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_` or `-`, else `OTHER`, checked by character codes (no regex in main);
        - `status` only on an `http` error, a whole number from 100 to 599.

        Why no `.` in a code, although the protocol takes one: a dotted code could be a host. Why not replace the characters, as `deviceName` does: what is left of a URL would still spell its host.
      - `stallEvent(at, report)`: `attempts` capped at 100.
    - **`ingest_done` and `epg_match` come from the data engine** (`apps/tv/src/data/engine.ts`), through a new option, `DataEngineOptions.onRefreshed(done)`: `{kind: 'catalog', source, result}` after each `refresh` that resolves, and `{kind: 'epg', result}` after each `refreshEpg` that resolves. Why there: every refresh goes through these two (the scheduler, onboarding's loader, the phone's add, a login or a link given again), as `onProfileChanged` already does.
      - **`ingest_done` for a catalog** (`catalogIngestEvent`):
        - `source` is `xtream`, or `m3u` for a link and for the phone's file;
        - `ms` is the result's `elapsedMs`, capped;
        - `bytes` is the bucket of `fetch.bytes`;
        - `items` is the bucket of the sum of `counts`.

        A result with no download (`fetch: null`: a 304) records nothing. Why: the event measures a download and its parse against their size; a 304 did neither, and would skew the times. An `unchanged` with the same bytes downloaded and parsed it all, so it counts.
      - **`ingest_done` for guides** (`epgIngestEvents`): one per outcome with a result and a download, with `source: 'epg'`, `ms`, `bytes`, and `items` the bucket of `summary.programmes` (the programmes read for the channels the guide feeds). Why `programmes` rather than `kept`: the ingest's work is what it read; `kept` depends on the window.
      - **Failed refreshes record nothing.** Why: the schema has no failure field, and a catalog's or a guide's failure is the provider's (a login, a server down); the app's own failures are crashes (33c). A failure count would be a later field of the schema.
      - **`epg_match`** after a `refreshEpg` whose result swapped a guide in (`committed`), deleted one or reranked one: the share of live channels with a guide, `rateBucket(live - unmatched, live)`.
        - `live` is the sum of the stored sources' `counts.live` (`epgStatus().sources`);
        - `unmatched` is the "Channels without a guide" count over all of them (`unmatched({sources, offset: 0, limit: 0})`);
        - nothing is recorded when there is no live channel.

        Why the unmatched count: it is the union across guides that the user sees, built once per reload and shared with that screen, while `EpgStatus.matched` can still count a channel a newer catalog dropped. Why only after a change: an unchanged refresh would repeat the last rate.
      - **Main's handler** builds the events and records them; a count that fails is logged and records nothing.
    - **Sampling** stays the config's, per kind (33b1). **Budget:** main.js has 21.4 KB left on Tizen; each new main-side file is probed with `artifacts/babel-polys30b2.ts`.
    - **No media-job check:** a real first frame reaches the controller exactly as the fake engine's does, so the controller's unit tests cover `ttffMs` and `stall`. Plain e2e checks a refused play end to end.

- [x] 33c. **The crash handler** (`apps/tv`, the workers): main's `error` and `unhandledrejection`, each worker's errors, each stack scrubbed with the user's own logins; at most a few crashes a session, deduplicated; queued as `crash`. Its shape is written before building. Merged in leg 119 (PR #147).
  - **Facts found in leg 118** (for the shape):
    - **The schema** (`packages/protocol/src/telemetry.ts`): `{kind: 'crash', at, where: 'main' | 'worker', stack}`, the stack at most 4,000 characters; the server scrubs it again with the generic rules (33a).
    - **The scrubber** is `scrubText(text, known)` from `@lablo/protocol/scrub`: 25.8 KB of polyfills on Chromium 53, so never in main.js (main has 14.5 KB left on Tizen after 33b3). The telemetry worker is 66.2 KB of 150.
    - **A worker's uncaught error** reaches main only as the `Worker`'s `ErrorEvent` (`message`, `filename` a `blob:` URL, `lineno`, `colno`; no stack), which each `create*Client` turns into `client.fail(new Error('<X> worker failed: …'))`. A handler's own throw is an RPC error answer (`toWire`), which main already handles: not a crash. A worker's unhandled rejection is not forwarded to main.
  - **Shape, drafted in leg 118 while PR #146's CI ran** (the next leg checks it against the code before building, and writes down any change):
    - **Main records the raw text, the telemetry worker scrubs it before anything is stored.** Main's handler (`apps/tv/src/telemetry/crash-state.ts`, hook-free) builds the text: the error's `name: message`, then its `stack` (else `at <filename>:<line>:<col>` from the event), cut to 16,000 characters. It records `{kind: 'crash', at, where, stack}` through `telemetry.record` (dropped while off, as every event). The worker's `send` scrubs each crash's `stack` with `scrubText(stack, known)` before it appends to the queue, so an unscrubbed stack is never written to IndexedDB. `known` comes from the stored profile the worker reads anyway: each source's link, server, username and password, each guide's link, and their hosts. Why: the scrubber can't go in main, and a raw stack held in memory until the next send never leaves the TV unscrubbed.
    - **Main's events:** `window`'s `error` (skipping "Script error." with no detail: nothing to learn) and `unhandledrejection` (the reason's stack, else its `String`). Check on Chromium 53 that core-js's `Promise` still raises `unhandledrejection` (a staged probe page, as leg 100's).
    - **Workers' errors:** a new `DataEngineOptions.onWorkerError(message, filename, line, col)` called from each worker's `onerror` (query, ingest, EPG, config, pair, backup, telemetry), recorded with `where: 'worker'`; the scrubber turns the `blob:` frame into `worker:<line>:<col>`.
    - **At most 3 crashes a session, deduplicated** by the text's first line and first frame; a crash inside the handler is never recorded (a guard). Why 3: a crash loop must not fill the 200-event queue, and the first ones say the most.
    - **No UI.** The Settings switch and the consent note (33b3) already cover crash reports.

    - **A worker's unhandled rejection** reaches neither its `Worker` nor the window, on any of the four.

None carries a channel, programme, movie or category name. The one platform text that quotes data is newer V8's `JSON.parse` message (a few characters of the text); in main it parses only the platforms' own answers (AVPlay, luna, the webOS device file).

Why: that skips a muted "Script error." and Chromium 156's copy of a worker's error alike, so a worker's error is recorded once, from its own `Worker`. Why not cancel the worker's event instead: Playwright's `pageerror` would no longer see worker errors, and every e2e spec's `trackErrors` relies on it.
    2. **A worker's error names its worker, not its URL:** `DataEngineOptions.onWorkerError({worker, message, line, col})`, `worker` one of `query`, `ingest`, `epg`, `config`, `pair`, `backup`, `telemetry`. The text is its message, then `    at <worker>.worker.js:<line>:<col>`. Why: a `blob:` URL says nothing and seven bundles would all read `worker:<line>:<col>`; the name says which bundle the position is in, and main holds no URL at all. The engine sets it on each worker it starts (`watch`), after the client's own `onerror`, which still runs first (the RPC calls reject as before); a report that throws is caught and logged. The hello screen's worker (a diagnostics screen with its own error line) is not watched.

A value with no string `stack` (a thrown string, an old engine's `DOMException`) gives the event's `message` (or `Uncaught (in promise) ` and its `String`) and, for the window's error, `    at <filename>:<line>:<col>` from the event (the scrubber cuts a `file://` frame to `main.js:<line>:<col>`). A `String()` that throws gives `[unreadable]`.
    4. **Main cuts the text at 20,000 characters** (`CRASH_TEXT_MAX`), above the scrubber's 16,000-character pre-cut, so the scrubber's cut (which drops the token at the cut) is the one that decides: main never leaves half a login at its cut for the known-string rule to miss. A unit test pins the order of the two constants (main can't import the scrubber's: that module pulls its polyfills).

The guard: every handler runs inside `try`/`catch`, and a flag stops a crash raised while one is being recorded.
    8. **Not recorded:** a worker's unhandled rejection (nothing reaches main; the RPC layer already answers a handler's rejection as an error, which main handles), and `rejectionhandled` (a late `catch` after a rejection already recorded: rare, and only a false positive). Why: forwarding them would need a listener and a message in every worker; a later step can add it if the reports show a need.
    9. **The e2e** goes in `e2e/telemetry.spec.ts`: a throw from a timer and an unhandled rejection, raised in the page, each message holding the playlist's link and login, then `sendTelemetry()`: two `crash` events (`where: 'main'`), each beginning `Uncaught`, with nothing of the source in the body; a fourth throw of the same error records nothing more.     - **The schema** (`packages/protocol/src/diag.ts`, `diagReportSchema`, exported from the index): `{installId, platform, appVersion, report}`, the body at most 32 KB. `report` holds only buckets, codes, counts and the device's model and firmware (the `capability` rules):
      - the device: `tier`, `model`, `firmware`, `chromium`, the `capability` probes;
      - the sources, at most 10 in the user's order: `type` (`xtream`, `m3u`, `m3u-file`), its state (`ok`, `failing` with its error code, `needs-login`, `blocked`), `items` as a `countBucket`, the hours since the last good refresh as a bucket;
      - the guides: how many, each one's state and programme bucket, and the match `rateBucket`;
      - the config: `fresh`, `stale` or `none`, and how many rules apply;
      - the last plays' results (at most 5: engine, container, mode, error kind and code, as `play_result`);
      - the crashes still in the telemetry queue (at most 3, scrubbed stacks).

      No names, links, hosts or logins: the server scrubs every free text again.
    - **`POST /v1/diag`** (`apps/web/src/server/diag.ts`, `src/app/v1/diag/route.ts`): the rate limit `diag` (5 an hour per caller: a person asks for it); the body cap (413), JSON and the schema (400); the stacks scrubbed; a 6-character Crockford code, unique (made again on a collision, a few tries, else 503); one row; the answer `{code}`, with the TV's CORS and `no-store`. **Retention: 30 days**, swept a bounded number of rows at each post, as telemetry's. Why 30: support needs a report only while its ticket is open.
    - **The TV:** Settings' fifth row, "Send a report to support". OK sends, and the row then shows the code ("Your code: K7M2Q9. Give it to support."), in the code's own large type, until Settings closes; a failure says so ("Couldn't send it. Try again."). The work is the telemetry worker's: a new RPC `report({base, platform, appVersion, device})`, where main passes only what main knows (the tier, the platform's info, the probes) and the worker reads the rest from IndexedDB (sources' meta, the guides' status, the config's cached copy, the queue's crashes) and posts. Main's part is the row and one call, inside the budget.
    - **Open for the next leg (decide, with the why):** the install ID while the telemetry switch is off. One way: a report always carries a new random ID (never the telemetry one), so a report can't be tied to telemetry rows. A report is the user's own explicit act, so it is sent with the switch off too, and the row says what it sends.
    - **Done when:** protocol unit tests (the schema takes a full report and refuses a URL, host or name in each free field); `test:web` (`diag.web.test.ts`: a row per report, the code's alphabet and uniqueness under a forced collision, 413, 400, 429, the sweep at 30 days, no address in any row, the route's methods, preflight and 503); `e2e:web` (a post over HTTP); unit tests for the worker's `report` (fake-indexeddb, nothing of a source in the body) and the row; e2e in en and ar against the stand-in (the code shown, the post holding nothing of the source); the en-XA walk; screenshots; lint, `tsc`, `build:tv` sizes, `verify:engines` 53/68; mutation with the list written first; PR merged on green CI; docs.
  - **Checked against the code in leg 120** (the draft holds except for these, each with its why):
    1. **No install ID at all.** The report carries none, and a migration (`diag_report_no_install`) drops `DiagReport.installId` (nothing is in production, step 34 runs the first production migration). Why: the report is the user's own act and stands alone; with no ID it can't be tied to telemetry rows, to the same TV's other reports or to a later install, and support finds it by its code alone. A new random ID per report would only fill the column with noise. **It is sent with the telemetry switch off too:** the row says what it sends, and nothing goes without the user's OK. That decides leg 119's open choice.
    2. **A source's state is what the TV stores, not a failure code.** A catalog's failed refresh is never stored (only the sources screen's answer for its own saves holds one, for the session). So a source reports its `type` (`xtream`, `m3u`, `m3u-file`), `items` (`countBucket` of its `counts`), `checked` (an age bucket since `checkedAt`, else `updatedAt`; `null`: never) and `needs` (`login`, `link` or `null`, step 32d4's sources without their login). Why: a source not checked for days says "failing" well enough for support, and a stored failure would be a data-side change of its own.
    3. **No "blocked".** Why: support can't act on it, and it would tie the report to a blocked provider (the same reason a refused play records no `play_result`, step 33b2). The report's `config` (`fresh`, `cached`, `none`: the config schedule's last answer, which main holds) says whether the TV has a policy at all. "How many rules apply" is left out for the same reason.
    4. **A guide** (from `epg_meta`, every guide, the user's and the playlists' own, in rank order) reports `programmes` (`countBucket` of `summary.programmes`), `matched` (`rateBucket` of `summary.matched` over `summary.wanted`), `checked` (the age bucket since `checkedAt`) and `failure` (`EpgFailure`: its code as a token and its HTTP status, or `null`).
    5. **The events are main's, this session's:** telemetry's `record` keeps the last 10 `play_result`, `stall` and `crash` events in memory, whatever the switch (`recent()`), and only a report sends them. Why: the queue is empty minutes after a send, and empty while the switch is off. They are the telemetry events as they are, so the schema reuses `telemetryEventSchema`. The worker scrubs each crash's stack with the profile's known strings before the post (as `enqueue`), and the server scrubs again.
    6. **More for support, each cheap and nameless:** `at` (the TV's clock: against the row's `createdAt`, the clock's skew), `zoneMin` (the UTC offset in minutes: a guide an hour off is a top complaint), `locale` (`en` or `ar`), and `telemetry` (the switch). The device's part is `capability`'s fields (`tier`, `model`, `firmware`, `chromium`, `probes`), built by main from what it already has.
    7. **The age buckets** (`AGE_BUCKETS`: `<1h`, `1h+`, `6h+`, `1d+`, `3d+`, `7d+`, `30d+`; a negative age, a clock moved back, reads `<1h`) and the limits (`DIAG_BODY_MAX_BYTES` 32 KB, at most 10 sources, 10 guides, 10 events) go in the schema-free `@lablo/protocol/telemetry-buckets`, so the worker builds a report without zod.
    8. **The code:** 6 characters of Crockford's base32 (`pair.ts`'s `CROCKFORD`), each from one random byte's low 5 bits (256 is a multiple of 32, so each character is uniform): 30 bits. Made again on a unique clash up to 5 times, else 503. The answer is 201 `{code}`. There is no route that reads a report, so a guessed code shows nothing.
  - **Split in leg 120** (two legs' work, as 33b was) into:
    - 33d1: the schema and the API;
    - 33d2: the TV (main's `recent()`, the worker's `report`, Settings' fifth row).

    33d is ticked when both are.
